```
     1→mod chid;
     2→mod common;
     3→mod desc;
     4→mod finalize;
     5→mod list;
     6→mod show;
     7→
     8→use std::process::ExitCode;
     9→
    10→use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};
    11→
    12→#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
    13→#[command(version, about = "vc-x1: jj workspace tooling")]
    14→pub struct Cli {
    15→    #[command(subcommand)]
    16→    command: Commands,
    17→}
    18→
    19→#[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
    20→enum Commands {
    21→    /// Print the changeID for a revision
    22→    Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
    23→
    24→    /// Show full description of a commit
    25→    Desc(desc::DescArgs),
    26→
    27→    /// List commits in a jj repo
    28→    List(list::ListArgs),
    29→
    30→    /// Show commit details and diff summary
    31→    Show(show::ShowArgs),
    32→
    33→    /// Squash working copy into target commit
    34→    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),
    35→}
    36→
    37→fn main() -> ExitCode {
    38→    let cli = Cli::parse();
    39→
    40→    match cli.command {
    41→        Commands::Chid(args) => {
    42→            if let Err(e) = chid::chid(&args) {
    43→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    44→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    45→            }
    46→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    47→        }
    48→        Commands::Desc(args) => {
    49→            if let Err(e) = desc::desc(&args) {
    50→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    51→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    52→            }
    53→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    54→        }
    55→        Commands::List(args) => {
    56→            if let Err(e) = list::list(&args) {
    57→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    58→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    59→            }
    60→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    61→        }
    62→        Commands::Show(args) => {
    63→            if let Err(e) = show::show(&args) {
    64→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    65→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    66→            }
    67→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    68→        }
    69→        Commands::Finalize(args) => {
    70→            let opts = args.into_opts();
    71→            finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize entry");
    72→            match finalize::finalize(&opts) {
    73→                Ok(()) => {
    74→                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize exit ok");
    75→                    ExitCode::SUCCESS
    76→                }
    77→                Err(e) => {
    78→                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("main: finalize exit err={e}"));
    79→                    eprintln!("error: {e}");
    80→                    ExitCode::FAILURE
    81→                }
    82→            }
    83→        }
    84→    }
    85→}
    86→
    87→#[cfg(test)]

    92→

    94→        Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap()
    95→    }
    96→
    97→    fn parse_err(args: &[&str]) -> String {
    98→        Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap_err().to_string()
    99→    }

   479→    #[test]
   480→    fn finalize_all_opts() {
   481→        let cli = parse(&[
   482→            "vc-x1",
   483→            "finalize",
   484→            "--repo",
   485→            ".claude",
   486→            "--source",
   487→            "@",
   488→            "--target",
   489→            "@-",
   490→            "--bookmark",
   491→            "dev-0.14.0",
   492→            "--delay",
   493→            "2.5",
   494→            "--push",
   495→            "--log",
   496→            "/tmp/test.log",
   497→            "--detach",
   498→        ]);
   499→        if let Commands::Finalize(args) = cli.command {
   500→            assert_eq!(args.repo, PathBuf::from(".claude"));
   501→            assert_eq!(args.source, "@");
   502→            assert_eq!(args.target, "@-");
   503→            assert_eq!(args.bookmark, "dev-0.14.0");
   504→            assert_eq!(args.delay, 2.5);
   505→            assert!(args.push);
   506→            assert_eq!(args.log, Some(PathBuf::from("/tmp/test.log")));
   507→            assert!(args.detach);
   508→        } else {
   509→            panic!("expected Finalize");
   510→        }
   511→    }
   512→
   513→    #[test]
   514→    fn finalize_partial_opts() {
   515→        let cli = parse(&[
   516→            "vc-x1",
   517→            "finalize",
   518→            "--bookmark",
   519→            "main",
   520→            "--repo",
   521→            ".claude",
   522→            "--push",
   523→        ]);
   524→        if let Commands::Finalize(args) = cli.command {
   525→            assert_eq!(args.repo, PathBuf::from(".claude"));
   526→            assert_eq!(args.bookmark, "main");
   527→            assert!(args.push);
   528→        } else {
   529→            panic!("expected Finalize");
   530→        }
   531→    }
   532→
   533→    #[test]
   534→    fn finalize_missing_bookmark() {
   535→        let err = parse_err(&["vc-x1", "finalize"]);
   536→        assert!(err.contains("--bookmark"));
   537→    }
   538→

> AGENT

Here's the overview:

**vc-x1** — a Rust CLI tool (v0.19.0) for jj (Jujutsu) workspace tooling, using `clap` and `jj-lib`.

### Subcommands
| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `chid` | Print changeID for a revision |
| `desc` | Show full description of a commit |
| `list` | List commits in a jj repo |
| `show` | Show commit details and diff summary |
| `finalize` | Squash working copy into target commit (used for session-end cleanup) |

### Source files (`src/`)
`main.rs`, `chid.rs`, `common.rs`, `desc.rs`, `finalize.rs`, `list.rs`, `show.rs`

### Current state
- On an unnamed working copy (`pqxtxxpn`), parent is `main` at v0.19.0
- Uncommitted changes in `notes/chores-02.md`, `notes/done.md`, `notes/todo.md`
- Dual-repo setup: app repo at `.`, bot session repo at `.claude`

### Open todos
- `::` revision syntax for jj compatibility
- `-p`/`--parents`, `-c`/`--children` for asymmetric parent/child counts
- Integration tests for `finalize` using temp jj repos
- Fix `.claude` repo history (dev0–dev2 squash issue)

What would you like to work on?

### Summary
You're reorganizing todo.md by moving older completed items to done.md and cleaning up the file structure.

1. **Reference number collision in todo.md** — The show subcommand entries changed from `[17]` and `[18]` to just `[18]`, but the diff shows a messy merge on those lines:
   - Line 26: `[1718]` — looks like `[17]` wasn't cleanly replaced with `[18]`, it's concatenated
   - Line 27: same `[1718]` issue
   - Line 28: same `[1718]` issue
   
   These should be `[18]` only (since `[17]` is the separate "jj commit organization" entry on line 25).

2. **Two new todo items replaced the old one** — You removed the "Determine the organization of commits in jj" todo and added two new ones (`::` syntax, asymmetric parent/child counts). Just confirming that's intentional — the original item was effectively completed via 0.17.0/0.18.0 work?

4. **Minor**: todo.md `## Done` description changed from ending with `.` to no period. Trivial but inconsistent with done.md's style.

The `[1718]` issue on lines 26–28 of todo.md needs fixing before committing.

> AGENT

That's a nice quality-of-life feature — especially given your dual-repo workflow where you're constantly running the same command twice with different `-R` values.

A few thoughts:

**Scope**: For read-only subcommands (`chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`) this is straightforward — iterate over repos and print results. For `finalize` it's more complex and probably not worth supporting (finalize is already repo-specific with bookmark/push semantics).

**Output clarity**: With multiple repos you'd need to label which output comes from which repo. Something like a repo path header before each result block.

**CLI design**: clap supports repeatable args (`Vec<PathBuf>`) so `-R . -R .claude` would parse naturally. The default could remain a single `.`.

**Implementation**: Mostly a change in `common.rs` — the resolve/iterate logic would loop over repos. Each subcommand's entry point would need to handle the vec instead of a single path.

Want to tackle this as 0.20.0 instead of `::` syntax, or keep `::` as 0.20.0 and do multi-repo as 0.21.0?

> DEVELOPER

Not supported for finalize!

> AGENT

Agreed, finalize stays single-repo. Want to capture that constraint in the todo item, or is the current wording sufficient?

And the ordering question still stands: multi-repo as 0.20.0, or `::` first?

> DEVELOPER

It's fine in the implementation

> AGENT

Got it. So which is 0.20.0 — multi-repo or `::`?

> DEVELOPER

multi-repo

> AGENT

Sounds good. So the plan is:

1. **0.20.0** — multi-repo `-R` support for `chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`
2. **0.21.0** — `::` revision syntax

Single-step or multi-step for 0.20.0?

> DEVELOPER

Let's do plan for 0.20.0 and decide separately

## Comprehensive Report: `-R`/`--repo` Argument Across All Subcommands

### 1. **Argument Declaration Pattern**

All subcommands (chid, desc, list, show, finalize) declare the `repo` argument identically:

```rust
/// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
#[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
pub repo: PathBuf,
```

**Key characteristics:**
- **Type**: `PathBuf` (filesystem path)
- **Flag**: `-R` (short) and `--repo` (long)
- **Default**: `"."` (current directory)
- **Position in struct**: Each file places it as a field in the respective Args struct

### 2. **Args Struct Locations and Field Order**

| File | Struct Name | Repo Field Position | Additional Fields |
|------|-------------|---------------------|-------------------|
| `src/chid.rs` | `ChidArgs` | Line 24 | pos_rev, pos_count, revision, limit |
| `src/desc.rs` | `DescArgs` | Line 24 | pos_rev, pos_count, revision, limit |
| `src/list.rs` | `ListArgs` | Line 24 | pos_rev, pos_count, revision, limit |
| `src/show.rs` | `ShowArgs` | Line 71 | pos_rev, pos_count, revision, files, limit |
| `src/finalize.rs` | `FinalizeArgs` | Line 11 | source, target, delay, bookmark, push, log, detach, exec |

**Observation**: chid, desc, list, show all have identical structure with `repo` at the same position. Finalize differs significantly—it has no positional args or revision flags, and the `repo` appears earlier in the declaration order.

### 3. **Execution Flow: From Args → Repo Loading**

All subcommands (except finalize) follow the same pattern:

**In chid.rs (lines 37–53):**
```rust
pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let spec = common::resolve_spec(...);
    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;  // ← repo arg passed here
    let (ids, anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(...)?;
    // ... output formatting
}
```

This pattern repeats in `desc()`, `list()`, and `show()` with identical repo loading.

### 4. **Shared Repo Loading in common.rs**

**Core function (lines 259–271):**
```rust
pub fn load_repo(
    path: &Path,
) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();

    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;

    Ok((workspace, repo))
}
```

**Key design:**
- Takes a `&Path` (any path, typically from `args.repo`)
- Returns both `Workspace` and `Arc<ReadonlyRepo>`
- Uses jj's `Workspace::load()` which resolves repo at the given path
- Chains `.load_at_head()` to get the latest state
- `.block_on()` runs the async future synchronously

### 5. **Common Shared Logic in common.rs**

The following functions work with the loaded repo and workspace:

| Function | Lines | Purpose |
|----------|-------|---------|
| `resolve_revset()` | 274–317 | Parse and evaluate revset strings (e.g., "@", "..@", "all()") |
| `collect_ids()` | 206–257 | Gather CommitIDs based on dot notation (ancestors/descendants) |
| `resolve_spec()` | 73–118 | Merge positional args with flag args, respecting dot notation |
| `parse_dot_rev()` | 41–67 | Parse `..` notation into DotSpec with counts |
| `format_chid()` | 158–161 | Format only the changeID (12-char hex) |
| `format_commit_short()` | 164–175 | Format: changeID commitID first-line |
| `format_commit_full()` | 178–196 | Format: changeID commitID + full description |

### 6. **Output Formatting Per Subcommand**

| Subcommand | Format Function | Highlighting | Container |
|------------|-----------------|---------------|-----------|
| **chid** | `format_chid()` | Bold anchor only | Single lines |
| **desc** | `format_commit_full()` | Bold first line of anchor | Multi-line indented (4 spaces) |
| **list** | `format_commit_short()` | Bold anchor line | Single lines |
| **show** | `format_commit_short()` + header info | Bold ids line anchor | Multi-section with separator ("────") |
| **finalize** | N/A (not a read operation) | N/A | Executes jj squash commands |

**All use `common::bold()` or `common::bold_first_line()` to highlight the anchor commit (at index `anchor_index`).**

### 7. **Finalize Args Structure (Different Pattern)**

**FinalizeArgs (lines 7–44):**
```rust
#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct FinalizeArgs {
    #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
    pub repo: PathBuf,           // Line 11
    
    #[arg(long, default_value = "@")]
    pub source: String,          // Line 15
    
    #[arg(long, default_value = "@-")]
    pub target: String,          // Line 18
    
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0)]
    pub delay: f64,              // Line 22
    
    #[arg(long)]
    pub bookmark: String,        // Line 26 (required)
    
    #[arg(long)]
    pub push: bool,              // Line 30
    
    #[arg(long)]
    pub log: Option<PathBuf>,    // Line 34
    
    #[arg(long)]
    pub detach: bool,            // Line 38
    
    #[arg(long, hide = true)]
    pub exec: bool,              // Line 42 (internal)
}
```

**Key differences from other subcommands:**
- No positional args (`pos_rev`, `pos_count`)
- No revision flag (`-r/--revision`)
- All args are named flags (no short forms except `repo`)
- Converted to `FinalizeOpts` struct via `into_opts()` (lines 59–82)
- Bookmark is mandatory; all others have sensible defaults

### 8. **Test Coverage (main.rs)**

Tests verify:
- **Default values**: repo defaults to `"."` in all subcommands
- **Explicit values**: `-R <path>` correctly parsed to `PathBuf`
- **Combined usage**: `-R .claude` works alongside other flags
- **Finalize-specific**: `--repo` flag naming and positioning

Examples:
- Line 125: `chid -R .claude` → `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".claude")`
- Line 211: `desc -R /tmp` → `args.repo == PathBuf::from("/tmp")`
- Line 298: `list -R /some/path` → `args.repo == PathBuf::from("/some/path")`
- Line 388: `show -R .claude` → `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".claude")`
- Line 500: `finalize --repo .claude` → `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".claude")`

## Summary of Key Findings

1. **Consistent Arg Definition**: chid, desc, list, show all use `#[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]` 
2. **Shared Load Pattern**: All call `common::load_repo(&args.repo)` which returns `(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>)`
3. **Common Resolution**: Shared `resolve_revset()`, `collect_ids()`, and spec resolution in common.rs
4. **Formatting Differences**: Output format varies per subcommand (pure IDs vs full descriptions), but all use shared formatters
5. **Finalize Divergence**: Uses `--repo` flag only (no `-R` short form defined), no revision args, converted to `FinalizeOpts` internally

Now I have a complete picture of all four read-only subcommands and their shared structure. Here is the implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan: Multi-Repo Support (v0.20.0)

### Overview

All four read-only subcommands (chid, desc, list, show) share an identical pattern: a single `repo: PathBuf` field parsed via `-R`. The goal is to change this to accept multiple `-R` flags, loop over each repo, and label output when more than one repo is queried.

### Design Decisions

**1. Arg type change: `PathBuf` to `Vec<PathBuf>` with default**

clap's `Vec<T>` collects repeated flags naturally: `-R . -R .claude` produces `vec![".", ".claude"]`. However, `default_value = "."` on a `Vec<PathBuf>` would always inject `"."` even when the user supplies explicit values. The fix is to use an **empty default** and apply the `"."` default in code:

```rust
#[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
```

Then in the subcommand function:
```rust
let repos = if args.repos.is_empty() { vec![PathBuf::from(".")] } else { args.repos.clone() };
```

This is the cleanest clap pattern for "default when none given, otherwise use exactly what was passed."

**2. Output labeling**

When `repos.len() > 1`, print a bold header line before each repo's output:
```
==> . <==
xkmqpvtl

==> .claude <==
yrnotmks
```

This follows the well-known `head` / `tail` convention for multi-file output. When only one repo is given (the common case), no header is printed, preserving backward compatibility.

**3. Shared helper vs per-subcommand loop**

Add a helper function in `common.rs` that handles the repo iteration scaffold:

```rust
pub fn for_each_repo<F>(repos: &[PathBuf], mut body: F) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
where
    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>, bool) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,
```

The `bool` parameter is `multi` (whether to print headers). Each subcommand calls this helper instead of `load_repo` directly, keeping the subcommand code compact. The helper:
- Defaults empty `repos` to `vec!["."]`
- Prints the `==> path <==` header when `repos.len() > 1`
- Prints a blank line separator between repos (except before the first)
- Calls `load_repo` for each path
- Calls the closure with workspace, repo, and multi flag
- Collects errors (continue-on-error strategy, see below)

**4. Error handling: continue on error**

If one repo fails to load (or a revset fails within it), print the error to stderr prefixed with the repo path, and continue to the next repo. Return a non-zero exit code if any repo failed. This matches how `git` and `jj` handle errors in multi-target operations -- the user sees partial results plus clear error messages.

Implementation: the helper returns `Result<(), Vec<(PathBuf, Box<dyn Error>)>>` or simpler: accumulate a bool `had_error` and return `Err` with a summary if any failed.

**5. Test strategy**

- **CLI parsing tests** (in `src/main.rs`): Update existing tests that assert `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".")` to assert `args.repos == vec![]` (empty, since default is applied in code). Add new tests:
  - Single `-R .claude` produces `vec![".claude"]`
  - Multiple `-R . -R .claude` produces `vec![".", ".claude"]`
  - No `-R` produces empty vec (code defaults to `"."`)
- **Unit test for `for_each_repo`**: Hard to unit-test since it needs real jj repos, but the logic is thin (just loop + load + header).
- **Integration / manual tests**: Run `vc-x1 chid @ -R . -R .claude` against the actual project to verify labeled output.

### Step-by-Step Implementation

**Step 1: Version bump**

In `Cargo.toml`, change `version = "0.19.1"` to `version = "0.20.0"`.

**Step 2: Add `for_each_repo` helper to `common.rs`**

Add after `load_repo`:

```rust
/// Run a closure against each repo path, printing headers when multiple repos.
pub fn for_each_repo<F>(
    repos: &[PathBuf],
    mut body: F,
) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
where
    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,
{
    let repos = if repos.is_empty() {
        vec![PathBuf::from(".")]
    } else {
        repos.to_vec()
    };
    let multi = repos.len() > 1;
    let mut first = true;
    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();

    for repo_path in &repos {
        if multi {
            if !first { println!(); }
            println!("{}", bold(&format!("==> {} <==", repo_path.display())));
        }
        first = false;

        match load_repo(repo_path) {
            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {
                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {
                    eprintln!("error ({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
                    errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
                }
            }
            Err(e) => {
                eprintln!("error ({}): {e}", repo_path.display());
                errors.push(format!("{}: {e}", repo_path.display()));
            }
        }
    }

    if errors.is_empty() {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(format!("{} repo(s) had errors", errors.len()).into())
    }
}
```

**Step 3: Update `ChidArgs` in `src/chid.rs`**

Change:
```rust
#[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
pub repo: PathBuf,
```
To:
```rust
#[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
```

Update `chid()` to use `for_each_repo`:
```rust
pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
        args.pos_count,
        &args.revision,
        args.limit,
        "@",
    );

    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, |workspace, repo| {
        let (ids, anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
            let line = common::format_chid(&commit);
            if i == anchor_index {
                println!("{}", common::bold(&line));
            } else {
                println!("{line}");
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    })
}
```

**Step 4: Apply identical pattern to `desc.rs`, `list.rs`, `show.rs`**

Each gets the same `repos: Vec<PathBuf>` field change and wraps its body in `for_each_repo`. The internal formatting logic stays unchanged.

For `show.rs`, the `file_limit` parsing stays outside the loop (it's derived from args, not repo-specific).

**Step 5: Do NOT touch `finalize.rs`**

It keeps its single `repo: PathBuf`.

**Step 6: Update CLI tests in `src/main.rs`**

Tests that currently assert `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".")` need to assert `args.repos.is_empty()` or `args.repos == vec![]`. Tests that assert `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".claude")` become `args.repos == vec![PathBuf::from(".claude")]`.

Add new multi-repo parsing tests:
```rust
#[test]
fn chid_multi_repo() {
    let cli = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-R", ".", "-R", ".claude"]);
    if let Commands::Chid(args) = cli.command {
        assert_eq!(args.repos, vec![PathBuf::from("."), PathBuf::from(".claude")]);
    } else { panic!("expected Chid"); }
}
```

Add similar tests for desc, list, show.

**Step 7: Run pre-commit checklist**

1. `cargo fmt`
2. `cargo clippy`
3. `cargo test`
4. `cargo install --path .`
5. Manual test: `vc-x1 chid @ -R . -R .claude`

### Potential Challenges

- **Closure borrowing**: The `for_each_repo` closure borrows `spec` from the outer scope. Since `spec` is `Clone` and the fields are just `String`/`Option<usize>`, this works fine with shared references. No ownership issues.
- **show.rs separator lines**: `show.rs` already prints `────` separators between commits within a single repo. Between repos it would print the `==> path <==` header. The blank-line-then-header approach should visually separate these cleanly.
- **clap `long = "repo"`**: Setting `long = "repo"` explicitly ensures `--repo` still works (clap would otherwise derive `--repos` from the field name `repos`).

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs` - Add `for_each_repo` helper function
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/chid.rs` - Change `repo: PathBuf` to `repos: Vec<PathBuf>`, wrap body in `for_each_repo`
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs` - Same arg change plus wrap; most complex subcommand to verify
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/main.rs` - Update ~15 CLI parsing tests for new field name/type, add multi-repo tests
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/Cargo.toml` - Version bump to 0.20.0

     9→use jj_lib::merged_tree::TreeDiffIterator;
    10→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
    11→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
    12→use jj_lib::workspace::Workspace;
    13→use pollster::FutureExt;
    14→
    15→use crate::common;
    16→
    17→/// Parsed file limit: None (suppress), Some(n) (cap at n), or all.
    18→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    19→pub enum FileLimit {
    20→    /// Don't show changed files section at all.
    21→    None,
    22→    /// Show up to N files (with first/last split if truncated).
    23→    Cap(usize),
    24→    /// Show all files.
    25→    All,
    26→}
    27→
    28→impl FileLimit {
    29→    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
    30→        match s {
    31→            "0" => Ok(FileLimit::None),
    32→            "all" => Ok(FileLimit::All),
    33→            _ => s
    34→                .parse::<usize>()
    35→                .map(FileLimit::Cap)
    36→                .map_err(|_| format!("invalid file limit '{s}': expected number, 0, or 'all'")),
    37→        }
    38→    }
    39→}
    40→
    41→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    42→pub struct ShowArgs {
    43→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    44→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    45→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    46→
    47→    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
    48→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    49→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    50→
    51→    /// Revision to show (default: @)
    52→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    53→    pub revision: String,
    54→
    55→    /// Max changed files: number, 0 (none), or 'all' (default: 50)
    56→    #[arg(short = 'f', long = "files", default_value = "50")]
    57→    pub files: String,
    58→
    59→    /// Number of commits to show
    60→    #[arg(
    61→        short = 'n',
    62→        long = "commits",
    63→        alias = "limit",
    64→        short_alias = 'l',
    65→        value_name = "COMMITS"
    66→    )]
    67→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    68→
    69→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    70→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    71→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    72→}
    73→
    74→pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    75→    let file_limit = FileLimit::parse(&args.files)?;
    76→
    77→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    78→
    79→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
    80→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    81→        args.pos_count,
    82→        &args.revision,
    83→        args.limit,
    84→        "@",
    85→    );
    86→
    87→    let (ids, anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(
    88→        &workspace,
    89→        &repo,
    90→        &spec.rev,
    91→        spec.desc_count,
    92→        spec.anc_count,
    93→    )?;
    94→
    95→    let mut first = true;
    96→    for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
    97→        if !first {
    98→            println!("────────────────────────────────────────");
    99→        }
   100→        first = false;
   101→
   102→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   103→        show_one_commit(&commit, &workspace, &repo, file_limit, i == anchor_index)?;
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    Ok(())
   107→}
   108→
   109→fn show_one_commit(
   110→    commit: &Commit,
   111→    workspace: &Workspace,
   112→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   113→    file_limit: FileLimit,
   114→    is_primary: bool,
   115→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   116→    // Author
   117→    let author = commit.author();
   118→    println!(
   119→        "Author:    {} <{}> ({})",
   120→        author.name,
   121→        author.email,
   122→        format_timestamp(&author.timestamp)
   123→    );
   124→
   125→    // Committer
   126→    let committer = commit.committer();
   127→    println!(
   128→        "Committer: {} <{}> ({})",
   129→        committer.name,
   130→        committer.email,
   131→        format_timestamp(&committer.timestamp)
   132→    );
   133→
   134→    // Ids
   135→    let ids_line = common::format_commit_short(commit);
   136→    if is_primary {
   137→        println!("Ids:       {}", common::bold(&ids_line));
   138→    } else {
   139→        println!("Ids:       {ids_line}");
   140→    }
   141→
   142→    // Parents
   143→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   144→    for parent_id in commit.parent_ids() {
   145→        if *parent_id == root_commit_id {
   146→            continue;
   147→        }
   148→        let parent = repo.store().get_commit(parent_id)?;
   149→        println!("Parent:    {}", common::format_commit_short(&parent));
   150→    }
   151→
   152→    // Children
   153→    let children_ids =
   154→        common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("children({})", commit.id().hex()))?;
   155→    for child_id in &children_ids {
   156→        if *child_id == root_commit_id {
   157→            continue;
   158→        }
   159→        let child = repo.store().get_commit(child_id)?;
   160→        println!("Child:     {}", common::format_commit_short(&child));
   161→    }
   162→
   163→    // Branches
   164→    let branches_str = format_branches(commit, workspace, repo)?;
   165→    println!("Branches:  {branches_str}");
   166→
   167→    // Follows
   168→    let follows = find_nearest_tag(commit, workspace, repo, true)?;
   169→    println!("Follows:   {}", follows.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
   170→
   171→    // Precedes
   172→    let precedes = find_nearest_tag(commit, workspace, repo, false)?;
   173→    println!("Precedes:  {}", precedes.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
   174→
   175→    // Description (body only — title is on the Ids line)
   176→    print_description(commit);
   177→
   178→    // Changed files
   179→    if file_limit != FileLimit::None {
   180→        print_diff(commit, repo, file_limit)?;
   181→    }
   182→
   183→    Ok(())
   184→}
   185→
   186→fn find_nearest_tag(
   187→    commit: &Commit,
   188→    workspace: &Workspace,
   189→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   190→    ancestor: bool,
   191→) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   192→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   193→
   194→    let revset_str = if ancestor {
   195→        format!("heads(tags() & ::{})", commit_hex)
   196→    } else {
   197→        format!("roots(tags() & {}::)", commit_hex)
   198→    };
   199→
   200→    let tag_ids = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;
   201→    if tag_ids.is_empty() {
   202→        return Ok(None);
   203→    }
   204→
   205→    let tag_id = &tag_ids[0];
   206→    let view = repo.view();
   207→    for (name, local_remote) in view.tags() {
   208→        if local_remote.local_target.added_ids().any(|id| id == tag_id) {
   209→            return Ok(Some(name.as_str().to_string()));
   210→        }
   211→    }
   212→
   213→    Ok(None)
   214→}
   215→
   216→fn format_branches(
   217→    commit: &Commit,
   218→    workspace: &Workspace,
   219→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   220→) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   221→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   222→    let view = repo.view();
   223→    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   224→
   225→    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {
   226→        for bookmark_id in local_remote.local_target.added_ids() {
   227→            let revset_str = format!("{} & ::{}", commit_hex, bookmark_id.hex());
   228→            let results = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;
   229→            if !results.is_empty() {
   230→                names.push(name.as_str().to_string());
   231→                break;
   232→            }
   233→        }
   234→
   235→        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {
   236→            if remote_name.as_str() == "git" {
   237→                continue;
   238→            }
   239→            for bookmark_id in remote_ref.target.added_ids() {
   240→                let revset_str = format!("{} & ::{}", commit_hex, bookmark_id.hex());
   241→                let results = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;
   242→                if !results.is_empty() {
   243→                    names.push(format!("{}@{}", name.as_str(), remote_name.as_str()));
   244→                    break;
   245→                }
   246→            }
   247→        }
   248→    }
   249→
   250→    Ok(names.join(", "))
   251→}
   252→
   253→fn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {
   254→    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;
   255→    let secs = millis / 1000;
   256→    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;
   257→
   258→    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)
   259→        .unwrap_or_default()
   260→        .with_timezone(
   261→            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),
   262→        );
   263→
   264→    dt.format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S").to_string()
   265→}
   266→
   267→fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {
   268→    let desc = commit.description();
   269→    let body: String = desc
   270→        .lines()
   271→        .skip(1)
   272→        .skip_while(|l| l.is_empty())
   273→        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
   274→        .join("\n");
   275→
   276→    println!("Description:");
   277→    for line in body.lines() {
   278→        println!("    {line}");
   279→    }
   280→}
   281→
   282→fn print_diff(
   283→    commit: &Commit,
   284→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   285→    file_limit: FileLimit,
   286→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   287→    let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo.as_ref()).block_on()?;
   288→    let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   289→
   290→    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   291→
   292→    let mut lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   293→    for entry in diff_iter {
   294→        let diff = entry.values?;
   295→        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   296→        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   297→
   298→        if matches!(&before, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_))) || matches!(&after, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))
   299→        {
   300→            continue;
   301→        }
   302→
   303→        let path_str = entry.path.as_internal_file_string().to_string();
   304→
   305→        let line = match (before, after) {
   306→            (None, Some(value)) => {
   307→                format!("    Added {} {path_str}", file_type_str(&value))
   308→            }
   309→            (Some(_), None) => {
   310→                format!(
   311→                    "    Removed {} {path_str}",
   312→                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),
   313→                )
   314→            }
   315→            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {
   316→                format!("    Modified {} {path_str}", file_type_str(&value))
   317→            }
   318→            (None, None) => continue,
   319→        };
   320→        lines.push(line);
   321→    }
   322→
   323→    let total = lines.len();
   324→    let cap = match file_limit {
   325→        FileLimit::All | FileLimit::None => 0,
   326→        FileLimit::Cap(n) => n,
   327→    };
   328→    let show_all = cap == 0 || total <= cap;
   329→
   330→    if show_all {
   331→        println!("Changed files: {total}");
   332→        for line in &lines {
   333→            println!("{line}");
   334→        }
   335→    } else {
   336→        println!("Changed files: {total}, showing first {cap}");
   337→        for line in &lines[..cap] {
   338→            println!("{line}");
   339→        }
   340→    }
   341→
   342→    Ok(())
   343→}
   344→
   345→fn file_type_str(value: &TreeValue) -> &'static str {
   346→    match value {
   347→        TreeValue::File {
   348→            executable: false, ..
   349→        } => "regular file",
   350→        TreeValue::File {
   351→            executable: true, ..
   352→        } => "executable file",
   353→        TreeValue::Symlink(_) => "symlink",
   354→        TreeValue::Tree(_) => "tree",
   355→        TreeValue::GitSubmodule(_) => "git submodule",
   356→    }
   357→}
   358→
   359→fn file_type_str_from_merged(merged: &jj_lib::merge::Merge<Option<TreeValue>>) -> &'static str {
   360→    if let Some(Some(value)) = merged.as_resolved() {
   361→        file_type_str(value)
   362→    } else {
   363→        "file"
   364→    }
   365→}
   366→
```

> AGENT

Good idea — that's more ergonomic than repeating `-R`. We can support both:

- `-R . -R .claude` (repeated flag)
- `-R .,.claude` (comma-separated)

Implementation-wise, this is a small addition: after clap collects the `Vec<PathBuf>`, we split each entry on commas and flatten. Something like:

```rust
let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = args.repos
    .iter()
    .flat_map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().split(',').map(PathBuf::from).collect::<Vec<_>>())
    .collect();
```

This means all of these work:
- `vc-x1 chid` → defaults to `.`
- `vc-x1 chid -R .claude` → single repo
- `vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude` → two repos via repeated flag
- `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude` → two repos via comma

The splitting logic would live in `for_each_repo` so each subcommand gets it for free.

Want me to update the plan with this and proceed?

## Approved Plan:
# Multi-Repo Support (0.20.0)

## Context

The user constantly runs chid/desc/list/show twice with `-R .` and `-R .claude` due to the dual-repo workflow. Allowing multiple `-R` flags eliminates this repetition.

## Scope

- **In scope**: chid, desc, list, show
- **Out of scope**: finalize (stays single `repo: PathBuf`)

## Plan

### 1. Bump version to 0.20.0

`Cargo.toml` version field.

### 2. Change `repo: PathBuf` to `repos: Vec<PathBuf>` in chid, desc, list, show

```rust
#[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
```

- No `default_value` — empty vec means "use `.`" (applied in code)
- `long = "repo"` keeps `--repo` flag name (clap would derive `--repos` otherwise)

### 3. Add `for_each_repo` helper in `common.rs`

- Splits each entry on commas and flattens, so `-R .,.claude` and `-R . -R .claude` both work
- Defaults empty repos to `vec!["."]`
- When `repos.len() > 1`, prints bold `==> path <==` header before each repo's output, with blank line separator between repos
- Single repo: no header (backward compatible)
- On repo load/execution error: prints to stderr, continues to next repo, returns error at end

### 4. Wrap each subcommand body in `for_each_repo`

- `spec` and `file_limit` parsing stay outside the loop (arg-derived, not repo-specific)
- Inner closure receives `(workspace, repo)` and runs the existing logic

### 5. Update tests in `main.rs`

- Existing `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".")` becomes `args.repos.is_empty()`
- Existing `args.repo == PathBuf::from(".claude")` becomes `args.repos == vec![PathBuf::from(".claude")]`
- Add multi-repo parsing tests: `-R . -R .claude` and `-R .,.claude`
- finalize tests unchanged

### 6. Pre-commit checklist

`cargo fmt` / `cargo clippy` / `cargo test` / `cargo install --path .` / manual test with `-R . -R .claude`

## Files to modify

- `Cargo.toml` — version bump
- `src/common.rs` — add `for_each_repo`
- `src/chid.rs` — args + wrap body
- `src/desc.rs` — args + wrap body
- `src/list.rs` — args + wrap body
- `src/show.rs` — args + wrap body
- `src/main.rs` — update/add tests

```

     1→use std::collections::HashMap;
     2→use std::path::Path;

    10→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
    11→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo, StoreFactories};
    12→use jj_lib::repo_path::RepoPathUiConverter;

    14→    RevsetAliasesMap, RevsetDiagnostics, RevsetExtensions, RevsetParseContext,
    15→    RevsetWorkspaceContext, SymbolResolver,
    16→};

    20→
    21→/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
    22→///
    23→/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
    24→/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
    25→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    26→pub struct DotSpec {
    27→    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
    28→    pub rev: String,
    29→    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
    30→    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
    31→    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
    32→    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
    36→///
    37→/// Returns the bare revision with open/closed counts per side.
    38→/// `None` means the side had dots (open for expansion);
    39→/// `Some(0)` means no dots on that side (closed).
    40→/// Actual counts are applied later from positional args.
    41→pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
    42→    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
    43→        DotSpec {
    44→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    45→            desc_count: None,
    46→            anc_count: None,
    47→        }
    48→    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
    49→        DotSpec {
    50→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    51→            desc_count: None,
    52→            anc_count: Some(0),
    53→        }
    54→    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
    55→        DotSpec {
    56→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    57→            desc_count: Some(0),
    58→            anc_count: None,
    59→        }
    60→    } else {
    61→        DotSpec {
    62→            rev: rev.to_string(),
    63→            desc_count: Some(0),
    64→            anc_count: Some(0),
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→}
    68→
    69→/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
    70→///
    71→/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
    72→/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
    73→pub fn resolve_spec(
    74→    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
    75→    pos_count: Option<usize>,
    76→    flag_rev: &str,
    77→    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
    78→    default_rev: &str,
    79→) -> DotSpec {
    80→    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
    81→    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
    82→        flag_rev
    83→    } else {
    84→        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev)
    85→    };
    86→    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
    87→
    88→    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
    89→        flag_limit
    90→    } else {
    91→        pos_count
    92→    };
    93→
    94→    if let Some(c) = count {
    95→        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
    96→        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
    97→        if both_open {
    98→            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
    99→            let desc_n = n / 2;
   100→            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
   101→            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
   102→            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
   103→        } else {
   104→            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
   105→                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
   106→            }
   107→            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
   108→                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
   109→            }
   110→            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
   111→            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
   112→                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
   113→            }
   114→        }
   115→    }
   116→
   117→    spec
   118→}
   119→
   120→/// Wrap text in ANSI bold escape codes.
   121→pub fn bold(s: &str) -> String {
   122→    format!("\x1b[1m{s}\x1b[0m")
   123→}
   124→
   125→/// Bold only the first line of a multi-line string.
   126→pub fn bold_first_line(s: &str) -> String {
   127→    if let Some(pos) = s.find('\n') {
   128→        format!("\x1b[1m{}\x1b[0m{}", &s[..pos], &s[pos..])
   129→    } else {
   130→        bold(s)
   131→    }
   132→}
   133→
   134→/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
   135→///
   136→/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
   137→pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
   138→    if n == 0 {
   139→        return s.to_string();
   140→    }
   141→    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
   142→    let mut lines = s.lines();
   143→    let mut result = match lines.next() {
   144→        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
   145→        None => return String::new(),
   146→    };
   147→    for line in lines {
   148→        result.push('\n');
   149→        if !line.is_empty() {
   150→            result.push_str(&pad);
   151→        }
   152→        result.push_str(line);
   153→    }
   154→    result
   155→}
   156→
   157→/// Format: just the short changeID.
   158→pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   159→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   160→    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
   161→}
   162→
   163→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line (single line).
   164→pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   165→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   166→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   167→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   168→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   169→    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   170→    if first_line.is_empty() {
   171→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   172→    } else {
   173→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
   174→    }
   175→}
   176→
   177→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.
   178→pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   179→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   180→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   181→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   182→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   183→    let desc = commit.description();
   184→    if desc.is_empty() {
   185→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   186→    } else {
   187→        let mut lines = desc.lines();
   188→        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("");
   189→        let mut result = format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}");
   190→        for line in lines {
   191→            result.push('\n');
   192→            result.push_str(line);
   193→        }
   194→        result
   195→    }
   196→}
   197→
   198→/// Collect commit IDs for `..x..` (both directions) or any dot notation.
   199→///
   200→/// Returns `(commit_ids, anchor_index)` — the IDs in display order
   201→/// (descendants closest to anchor, anchor, ancestors closest to anchor)
   202→/// and the index of the anchor commit within the list.
   203→///
   204→/// - `desc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest descendants, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
   205→/// - `anc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest ancestors, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none
   206→pub fn collect_ids(
   207→    workspace: &Workspace,
   208→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   209→    rev: &str,
   210→    desc_count: Option<usize>,
   211→    anc_count: Option<usize>,
   212→) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   213→    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, rev)?;
   214→    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
   215→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{rev}'").into());
   216→    }
   217→    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
   218→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   219→
   220→    // Descendants (closest to anchor)
   221→    let mut desc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
   222→    if desc_count != Some(0) {
   223→        let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("{rev}::"))?;
   224→        desc_ids = descendant_ids
   225→            .into_iter()
   226→            .filter(|id| *id != root_commit_id && *id != *anchor_id)
   227→            .collect();
   228→        if let Some(n) = desc_count {
   229→            let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(n);
   230→            desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();
   231→        }
   232→    }
   233→
   234→    // Ancestors (closest to anchor)
   235→    let mut anc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();
   236→    if anc_count != Some(0) {
   237→        let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("::{rev}"))?;
   238→        let limit = anc_count.unwrap_or(usize::MAX);
   239→        let mut count = 0;
   240→        for commit_id in ancestor_ids {
   241→            if commit_id == root_commit_id || commit_id == *anchor_id {
   242→                continue;
   243→            }
   244→            anc_ids.push(commit_id);
   245→            count += 1;
   246→            if count >= limit {
   247→                break;
   248→            }
   249→        }
   250→    }
   251→
   252→    let anchor_index = desc_ids.len();
   253→    let mut result = desc_ids;
   254→    result.push(anchor_id.clone());
   255→    result.extend(anc_ids);
   256→    Ok((result, anchor_index))
   257→}
   258→
   259→pub fn load_repo(
   260→    path: &Path,
   261→) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   262→    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
   263→    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
   264→    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
   265→    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
   266→
   267→    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
   268→    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
   269→
   270→    Ok((workspace, repo))
   271→}
   272→
   273→/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
   274→pub fn resolve_revset(
   275→    workspace: &Workspace,
   276→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   277→    revset_str: &str,
   278→) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   279→    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
   280→    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
   281→    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
   282→    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
   283→    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
   284→        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
   285→        base: workspace_root,
   286→    };
   287→    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
   288→    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
   289→        path_converter: &path_converter,
   290→        workspace_name,
   291→    };
   292→    let context = RevsetParseContext {
   293→        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
   294→        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
   295→        user_email: "",
   296→        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
   297→        default_ignored_remote: None,
   298→        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
   299→        use_glob_by_default: false,
   300→        extensions: &extensions,
   301→        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
   302→    };
   303→
   304→    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
   305→    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
   306→
   307→    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
   308→    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
   309→    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
   310→    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
   311→
   312→    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
   313→    for result in revset.iter() {
   314→        commit_ids.push(result?);
   315→    }
   316→    Ok(commit_ids)
   317→}
   318→
   319→#[cfg(test)]
   320→mod tests {
   321→    use super::*;
   322→
   323→    #[test]
   324→    fn parse_dot_rev_bare() {
   325→        let spec = parse_dot_rev("@");
   326→        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "@");
   327→        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, Some(0));
   328→        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, Some(0));
   329→    }
   330→
   331→    #[test]
   332→    fn parse_dot_rev_ancestors() {
   333→        let spec = parse_dot_rev("@..");
   334→        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "@");
   335→        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, Some(0));
   336→        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, None);
   337→    }
   338→
   339→    #[test]
   340→    fn parse_dot_rev_descendants() {
   341→        let spec = parse_dot_rev("..@");
   342→        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "@");
   343→        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, None);
   344→        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, Some(0));
   345→    }
   346→
   347→    #[test]
   348→    fn parse_dot_rev_both() {
   349→        let spec = parse_dot_rev("..@..");
   350→        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "@");
   351→        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, None);
   352→        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, None);
   353→    }
   354→
   355→    #[test]
   356→    fn parse_dot_rev_changeid() {
   357→        let spec = parse_dot_rev("abcd..");
   358→        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "abcd");
   359→        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, Some(0));
   360→        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, None);
   361→    }
   362→
   363→    #[test]
   364→    fn parse_dot_rev_both_changeid() {
   365→        let spec = parse_dot_rev("..abcd..");
   366→        assert_eq!(spec.rev, "abcd");
   367→        assert_eq!(spec.desc_count, None);
   368→        assert_eq!(spec.anc_count, None);
   369→    }
   370→
   371→    #[test]
   372→    fn resolve_spec_defaults() {
   373→        let s = resolve_spec(None, None, "@", None, "@");
   374→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   375→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(0));
   376→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(0));
   377→    }
   378→
   379→    #[test]
   380→    fn resolve_spec_bare_with_count() {
   381→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("@"), Some(5), "@", None, "@");
   382→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   383→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(0));
   384→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(4)); // 5 - 1 = 4 ancestors
   385→    }
   386→
   387→    #[test]
   388→    fn resolve_spec_ancestors() {
   389→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("@.."), Some(3), "@", None, "@");
   390→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   391→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(0));
   392→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(2)); // 3 - 1 = 2 ancestors
   393→    }
   394→
   395→    #[test]
   396→    fn resolve_spec_descendants() {
   397→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("..@"), Some(3), "@", None, "@");
   398→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   399→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(2)); // 3 - 1 = 2 descendants
   400→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(0));
   401→    }
   402→
   403→    #[test]
   404→    fn resolve_spec_both() {
   405→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("..@.."), Some(5), "@", None, "@");
   406→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   407→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(2)); // 4/2 = 2 descendants
   408→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(2)); // 4-2 = 2 ancestors
   409→    }
   410→
   411→    #[test]
   412→    fn resolve_spec_both_odd() {
   413→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("..@.."), Some(4), "@", None, "@");
   414→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   415→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(1)); // 3/2 = 1 descendant
   416→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(2)); // 3-1 = 2 ancestors (extra goes to ancestors)
   417→    }
   418→
   419→    #[test]
   420→    fn resolve_spec_flag_overrides_positional() {
   421→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("@.."), Some(5), "@-", None, "@");
   422→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@-"); // flag_rev takes precedence
   423→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(0));
   424→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(4)); // pos_count 5 - 1
   425→    }
   426→
   427→    #[test]
   428→    fn resolve_spec_ancestors_no_count() {
   429→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("@.."), None, "@", None, "@");
   430→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   431→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(0));
   432→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, None); // unlimited
   433→    }
   434→
   435→    #[test]
   436→    fn resolve_spec_flag_limit_overrides_pos_count() {
   437→        let s = resolve_spec(Some("@.."), Some(5), "@", Some(3), "@");
   438→        assert_eq!(s.rev, "@");
   439→        assert_eq!(s.desc_count, Some(0));
   440→        assert_eq!(s.anc_count, Some(2)); // flag 3 - 1 = 2
   441→    }
   442→
   443→    #[test]
   444→    fn indent_body_zero() {
   445→        let s = "first\nsecond\nthird";
   446→        assert_eq!(indent_body(s, 0), s);
   447→    }
   448→
   449→    #[test]
   450→    fn indent_body_single_line() {
   451→        assert_eq!(indent_body("only", 3), "only");
   452→    }
   453→
   454→    #[test]
   455→    fn indent_body_multi_line() {
   456→        let s = "first\nsecond\nthird";
   457→        assert_eq!(indent_body(s, 3), "first\n   second\n   third");
   458→    }
   459→
   460→    #[test]
   461→    fn indent_body_empty_lines_preserved() {
   462→        let s = "first\n\nthird";
   463→        assert_eq!(indent_body(s, 3), "first\n\n   third");
   464→    }
   465→
   466→    #[test]
   467→    fn indent_body_empty_string() {
   468→        assert_eq!(indent_body("", 3), "");
   469→    }
   470→}
   471→

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→
     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
     5→
     6→use crate::common;
     7→
     8→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
     9→pub struct ListArgs {
    10→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    11→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    12→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    13→
    14→    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
    15→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    16→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    17→
    18→    /// Revision to list (default: @)
    19→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    20→    pub revision: String,
    21→
    22→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    24→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    25→
    26→    /// Number of commits to show
    27→    #[arg(
    28→        short = 'n',
    29→        long = "commits",
    30→        alias = "limit",
    31→        short_alias = 'l',
    32→        value_name = "COMMITS"
    33→    )]
    34→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    35→}
    36→
    37→pub fn list(args: &ListArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    38→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
    39→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    40→        args.pos_count,
    41→        &args.revision,
    42→        args.limit,
    43→        "@",
    44→    );
    45→
    46→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    47→    let (ids, anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(
    48→        &workspace,
    49→        &repo,
    50→        &spec.rev,
    51→        spec.desc_count,
    52→        spec.anc_count,
    53→    )?;
    54→
    55→    for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
    56→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    57→        let line = common::format_commit_short(&commit);
    58→        if i == anchor_index {
    59→            println!("{}", common::bold(&line));
    60→        } else {
    61→            println!("{line}");
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→
    65→    Ok(())
    66→}
    67→

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn load_repo(",
  "new_string": "/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `[\".\"]`, and run a closure for each.\n///\n/// Prints bold `==> path <==` headers when multiple repos are queried.\n/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.\npub fn for_each_repo<F>(\n    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],\n    mut body: F,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,\n{\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {\n        vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]\n    } else {\n        raw_repos\n            .iter()\n            .flat_map(|p| {\n                p.to_string_lossy()\n                    .split(',')\n                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n            })\n            .collect()\n    };\n    let multi = repos.len() > 1;\n    let mut first = true;\n    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for repo_path in &repos {\n        if multi {\n            if !first {\n                println!();\n            }\n            println!(\"{}\", bold(&format!(\"==> {} <==\", repo_path.display())));\n        }\n        first = false;\n\n        match load_repo(repo_path) {\n            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {\n                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {\n                    eprintln!(\"error ({}): {e}\", repo_path.display());\n                    errors.push(format!(\"{}: {e}\", repo_path.display()));\n                }\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                eprintln!(\"error ({}): {e}\", repo_path.display());\n                errors.push(format!(\"{}: {e}\", repo_path.display()));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if errors.is_empty() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(format!(\"{} repo(s) had errors\", errors.len()).into())\n    }\n}\n\npub fn load_repo("
}

}

  "new_string": "pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let file_limit = FileLimit::parse(&args.files)?;\n\n    let spec = common::resolve_spec(\n        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n        args.pos_count,\n        &args.revision,\n        args.limit,\n        \"@\",\n    );\n\n    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, |workspace, repo| {\n        let (ids, anchor_index) =\n            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;\n\n        let mut first = true;\n        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {\n            if !first {\n                println!(\"────────────────────────────────────────\");\n            }\n            first = false;\n\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n            show_one_commit(&commit, workspace, repo, file_limit, i == anchor_index)?;\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    })\n}"
}

```
     1→# vc-x1
     2→
     3→- [Overview](#vc-x1)
     4→- [Usage](#usage)
     5→  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)
     6→  - [finalize](#finalize)
     7→  - [Testing finalize](#testing-finalize)
     8→- [Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers](#cross-repo-linking-with-git-trailers)
     9→- [jj Tips for Git Users](#jj-tips-for-git-users)
    10→- [License](#license)
    11→
    12→This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
    13→We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
    14→in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
    15→initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
    16→made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
    17→and one with the conversation with the bot.
    18→
    19→I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
    20→each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
    21→of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
    22→changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
    23→between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
    24→evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
    25→most importantly **why** the code evolved.
    26→
    27→The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
    28→(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
    29→existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
    30→own IDE for vc.
    31→
    32→See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
    33→for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
    34→doing so and I then created this README.md file.
    35→
    36→## Usage
    37→
    38→```
    39→vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
    40→vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
    41→vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
    42→vc-x1 show [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show commit details and diff summary
    43→vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]   # Squash working copy into target
    44→vc-x1 --version                        # Print version
    45→vc-x1 --help                           # Print help
    46→```
    47→
    48→**The `..` notation:** dots on the revision show which direction to
    49→list commits:
    50→
    51→- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
    52→- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
    53→- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
    54→
    55→COMMITS is the total number of commits to show (x is always included).
    56→Without dots, `x` with a count defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
    57→For `..x..`, the budget is split: ancestors get the extra on odd counts.
    58→
    59→```
    60→vc-x1 list -r @              # just @ (1 commit)
    61→vc-x1 list -r @.. -n 5       # @ + 4 ancestors (5 commits, @ at top)
    62→vc-x1 list -r ..@ -n 3       # 2 descendants + @ (3 commits, @ at bottom)
    63→vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 3     # 1 descendant, @, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
    64→vc-x1 list -r ..@.. -n 4     # 1 descendant, @, 2 ancestors (4 commits)
    65→```
    66→
    67→### Positional shorthand
    68→
    69→REVISION and COMMITS can be given as positional arguments, omitting
    70→`-r` and `-n`:
    71→
    72→```
    73→vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
    74→vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 4 ancestors (5 commits)
    75→vc-x1 list x.. 5            # same as above
    76→vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 2 descendants + x (3 commits)
    77→vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 1 descendant, x, 1 ancestor (3 commits)
    78→vc-x1 list ..x.. 1          # just x (1 commit)
    79→```
    80→
    81→**Defaults:** with no arguments, REVISION is `@` and COMMITS is 1
    82→(just the working copy).
    83→
    84→Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-n`/`--commits`, and `-R`/`--repo`
    85→take precedence over positional arguments.
    86→
    87→### finalize
    88→
    89→Solves the session repo coherence problem: when the bot commits `.claude`,
    90→the act of committing generates more session data. `finalize` waits,
    91→then squashes the trailing writes into the target commit.
    92→
    93→By default finalize runs in the foreground. Use `--detach` to run in
    94→the background (the bot uses this so the session can end immediately).
    95→
    96→The bot's last action in a session:
    97→
    98→```
    99→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark main --delay 10 --detach --push
   100→```
   101→
   102→If there is non-written session data after a session ends (e.g.
   103→finalize failed or was skipped), run it manually:
   104→
   105→```
   106→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark main --push
   107→```
   108→
   109→This runs in the foreground, squashes, advances the bookmark, and pushes.
   110→
   111→See [finalize subcommand](./notes/chores-01.md#finalize-subcommand-for-session-repo-coherence)
   112→for full option reference and design details.
   113→
   114→### Testing finalize
   115→
   116→Always test against a throwaway jj repo, never the live workspace.
   117→Use `mktemp -d` for unique directories so results can't be confused
   118→with a previous run:
   119→
   120→```bash
   121→# Create and init a temp repo
   122→dir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
   123→(cd "$dir" && jj git init)
   124→
   125→# Foreground (default, blocks, logs to test dir)
   126→vc-x1 finalize --repo "$dir" --bookmark main --log "$dir/finalize.log"
   127→cat "$dir/finalize.log"
   128→
   129→# Detached (returns immediately, child logs to test dir)
   130→dir2=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
   131→(cd "$dir2" && jj git init)
   132→vc-x1 finalize --detach --repo "$dir2" --bookmark main --log "$dir2/finalize.log"
   133→cat "$dir2/finalize.log"
   134→```
   135→
   136→The log file shows timestamped (nanoseconds) entries with PIDs, covering
   137→the full flow: `main` entry/exit, `finalize` entry/exit, `detach`
   138→spawn, and `finalize_exec` in the child process.
   139→
   140→## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
   141→
   142→Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
   143→to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
   144→(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
   145→and jj changeID:
   146→
   147→```
   148→ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
   149→ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
   150→```
   151→
   152→Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
   153→Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
   154→within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
   155→
   156→For full details see:
   157→- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
   158→  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
   159→  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
   160→  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
   161→
   162→## jj Tips for Git Users
   163→
   164→If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
   165→tools like `gitk --all`.
   166→
   167→### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
   168→
   169→jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
   170→When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
   171→jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
   172→undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
   173→shows the current version of each change.
   174→
   175→### Useful commands
   176→
   177→| Command | Description |
   178→|---------|-------------|
   179→| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
   180→| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
   181→| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
   182→| `jj obslog -r <change-id>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
   183→| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
   184→
   185→In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
   186→the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
   187→

   193→- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
   194→

   198→for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
   199→be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
   200→

Multi-repo is supported for\n`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.\n\n### finalize"

So when each repo returns just 1 result (the common `chid -R .,.claude` case), no headers — but when using `-n 3` and getting multiple results per repo, show headers? That would need buffering since we don't know the count until after running. 

Or did you mean something simpler?

> DEVELOPER

With the header I'm guessing you won't use it when you commit, as it's more effort parsing then just getting the two values. Another option is let the user decide if they want a seperator and what it should be?

> AGENT

Good point — a user-controlled separator flag is cleaner. Something like:

```
-S, --separator <TEXT>   Separator between repos (default: bold ==> path <==)
```

So:
- `-R .,.claude` with no `-S` → bold headers (current behavior)
- `-R .,.claude -S ""` → no separator at all, just raw output
- `-R .,.claude -S "---"` → custom separator

And for your commit workflow you'd use `-S ""` to get clean parseable output.

Does that feel right, or would you prefer a different default (like no separator by default)?

> DEVELOPER

how about default is seperator, -s <seperator-string>, -S flag is no seperator

> AGENT

Clean. So:

- `-R .,.claude` → bold `==> path <==` headers (default)
- `-R .,.claude -s "---"` → custom separator string between repos
- `-R .,.claude -S` → no separator, raw output

Let me implement that.

  "old_string": "/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `[\".\"]`, and run a closure for each.\n///\n/// Prints bold `==> path <==` headers when multiple repos are queried.\n/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.\npub fn for_each_repo<F>(\n    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],\n    mut body: F,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,\n{\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {\n        vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]\n    } else {\n        raw_repos\n            .iter()\n            .flat_map(|p| {\n                p.to_string_lossy()\n                    .split(',')\n                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n            })\n            .collect()\n    };\n    let multi = repos.len() > 1;\n    let mut first = true;\n    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for repo_path in &repos {\n        if multi {\n            if !first {\n                println!();\n            }\n            println!(\"{}\", bold(&format!(\"==> {} <==\", repo_path.display())));\n        }\n        first = false;\n\n        match load_repo(repo_path) {\n            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {\n                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {\n                    eprintln!(\"error ({}): {e}\", repo_path.display());\n                    errors.push(format!(\"{}: {e}\", repo_path.display()));\n                }\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                eprintln!(\"error ({}): {e}\", repo_path.display());\n                errors.push(format!(\"{}: {e}\", repo_path.display()));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if errors.is_empty() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(format!(\"{} repo(s) had errors\", errors.len()).into())\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Separator style between repos in multi-repo output.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum Separator {\n    /// Bold `==> path <==` header per repo (default).\n    Default,\n    /// Custom string between repos.\n    Custom(String),\n    /// No separator at all.\n    None,\n}\n\n/// Resolve `-s` / `-S` flags into a `Separator`.\n///\n/// `-S` (no separator) takes precedence over `-s` (custom separator).\npub fn resolve_separator(custom: &Option<String>, suppress: bool) -> Separator {\n    if suppress {\n        Separator::None\n    } else if let Some(s) = custom {\n        Separator::Custom(s.clone())\n    } else {\n        Separator::Default\n    }\n}\n\n/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `[\".\"]`, and run a closure for each.\n///\n/// Separator style controls output between repos.\n/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.\npub fn for_each_repo<F>(\n    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],\n    separator: &Separator,\n    mut body: F,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,\n{\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {\n        vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]\n    } else {\n        raw_repos\n            .iter()\n            .flat_map(|p| {\n                p.to_string_lossy()\n                    .split(',')\n                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n            })\n            .collect()\n    };\n    let multi = repos.len() > 1;\n    let mut first = true;\n    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for repo_path in &repos {\n        if multi {\n            match separator {\n                Separator::Default => {\n                    if !first {\n                        println!();\n                    }\n                    println!(\"{}\", bold(&format!(\"==> {} <==\", repo_path.display())));\n                }\n                Separator::Custom(s) => {\n                    if !first {\n                        println!(\"{s}\");\n                    }\n                }\n                Separator::None => {}\n            }\n        }\n        first = false;\n\n        match load_repo(repo_path) {\n            Ok((workspace, repo)) => {\n                if let Err(e) = body(&workspace, &repo) {\n                    eprintln!(\"error ({}): {e}\", repo_path.display());\n                    errors.push(format!(\"{}: {e}\", repo_path.display()));\n                }\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                eprintln!(\"error ({}): {e}\", repo_path.display());\n                errors.push(format!(\"{}: {e}\", repo_path.display()));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if errors.is_empty() {\n        Ok(())\n    } else {\n        Err(format!(\"{} repo(s) had errors\", errors.len()).into())\n    }\n}"
}

When multiple repos are given, output is\nlabeled with bold `==> path <==` headers:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated\nvc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands\n```\n\nWith a single repo (or no `-R`), no header is printed — backward\ncompatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for\n`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.",
  "new_string": "The `-R`/`--repo` flag can be repeated or comma-separated to query\nmultiple repos at once. When multiple repos are given, output is\nlabeled with bold `==> path <==` headers by default:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated\nvc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands\n```\n\nControl the separator between repos with `-s` and `-S`:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default: bold ==> path <== headers\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -s \"===\"   # custom separator string\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -S         # no separator (raw output)\n```\n\nWith a single repo (or no `-R`), no separator is printed — backward\ncompatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for\n`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo."
}
