> AGENT

68 tests pass. All checks green, binary installed and manually verified. The three separator modes all work:

- Default: bold `==> path <==` headers
- `-s "==="`: custom separator string
- `-S`: no separator (raw output)

Ready for your review.

> DEVELOPER

Hmm, so a -s xxx means no header but a seperator, a little odd

> AGENT

Yeah, it's two different things mixed together — the header (showing which repo) vs the separator (visual break between output). 

How about: the bold `==> path <==` header is always shown for multi-repo, and `-s`/`-S` only controls the separator *between* repos?

- Default: header + blank line between repos
- `-s "---"`: header + `---` between repos
- `-S`: header only, no extra separator

Or would you rather the header be optional too?

> DEVELOPER

I'm thinking -s xxx means the charcters in the header and -S means no header (which means no seperation)

> AGENT

Simple and clean. So `-s` customizes the header characters (replacing `==>` / `<==`), and `-S` kills the header entirely.

- `-R .,.claude` → `==> . <==` / `==> .claude <==` (default)
- `-R .,.claude -s ">>>"` → `>>> . <<<` / `>>> .claude <<<`

Wait — does `-s` replace the whole decoration, or the left/right arrows? Maybe simpler:

- `-s "---"` → `--- . ---` / `--- .claude ---`

Or did you mean `-s` is just a flat string with no path at all?

> DEVELOPER

I like the path and the simple repeated characters so you can't get ">>> name <<<" to bad :)

> AGENT

Right, so `-s` sets the decoration characters used on both sides around the path:

- Default: `==> . <==` / `==> .claude <==`
- `-s ">>>"`: `>>> . <<<` — wait, reversing doesn't generalize well for arbitrary strings

How about just using the same string on both sides?

- `-s "---"`: `--- . ---` / `--- .claude ---`
- `-s "***"`: `*** . ***` / `*** .claude ***`
- `-s "=>"`: `=> . =>` / `=> .claude =>`

That's predictable and simple. Work for you?

> DEVELOPER

y

  "old_string": "/// Separator style between repos in multi-repo output.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum Separator {\n    /// Bold `==> path <==` header per repo (default).\n    Default,\n    /// Custom string between repos.\n    Custom(String),\n    /// No separator at all.\n    None,\n}\n\n/// Resolve `-s` / `-S` flags into a `Separator`.\n///\n/// `-S` (no separator) takes precedence over `-s` (custom separator).\npub fn resolve_separator(custom: &Option<String>, suppress: bool) -> Separator {\n    if suppress {\n        Separator::None\n    } else if let Some(s) = custom {\n        Separator::Custom(s.clone())\n    } else {\n        Separator::Default\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum Header {\n    /// Bold `==> path <==` header per repo (default).\n    Default,\n    /// Custom decoration: `deco path deco`.\n    Custom(String),\n    /// No header at all.\n    None,\n}\n\n/// Resolve `-s` / `-S` flags into a `Header`.\n///\n/// `-S` (no header) takes precedence over `-s` (custom decoration).\npub fn resolve_header(custom: &Option<String>, suppress: bool) -> Header {\n    if suppress {\n        Header::None\n    } else if let Some(s) = custom {\n        Header::Custom(s.clone())\n    } else {\n        Header::Default\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `[\".\"]`, and run a closure for each.\n///\n/// Separator style controls output between repos.\n/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.\npub fn for_each_repo<F>(\n    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],\n    separator: &Separator,\n    mut body: F,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,\n{\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {\n        vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]\n    } else {\n        raw_repos\n            .iter()\n            .flat_map(|p| {\n                p.to_string_lossy()\n                    .split(',')\n                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n            })\n            .collect()\n    };\n    let multi = repos.len() > 1;\n    let mut first = true;\n    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for repo_path in &repos {\n        if multi {\n            match separator {\n                Separator::Default => {\n                    if !first {\n                        println!();\n                    }\n                    println!(\"{}\", bold(&format!(\"==> {} <==\", repo_path.display())));\n                }\n                Separator::Custom(s) => {\n                    if !first {\n                        println!(\"{s}\");\n                    }\n                }\n                Separator::None => {}\n            }\n        }\n        first = false;",
  "new_string": "/// Expand comma-separated repo paths, default to `[\".\"]`, and run a closure for each.\n///\n/// Header style controls output between repos.\n/// Continues past errors, printing them to stderr, and returns an error if any failed.\npub fn for_each_repo<F>(\n    raw_repos: &[PathBuf],\n    header: &Header,\n    mut body: F,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: FnMut(&Workspace, &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>,\n{\n    let repos: Vec<PathBuf> = if raw_repos.is_empty() {\n        vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]\n    } else {\n        raw_repos\n            .iter()\n            .flat_map(|p| {\n                p.to_string_lossy()\n                    .split(',')\n                    .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n            })\n            .collect()\n    };\n    let multi = repos.len() > 1;\n    let mut first = true;\n    let mut errors: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for repo_path in &repos {\n        if multi {\n            match header {\n                Header::Default => {\n                    if !first {\n                        println!();\n                    }\n                    println!(\"{}\", bold(&format!(\"==> {} <==\", repo_path.display())));\n                }\n                Header::Custom(deco) => {\n                    if !first {\n                        println!();\n                    }\n                    println!(\n                        \"{}\",\n                        bold(&format!(\"{deco} {} {deco}\", repo_path.display()))\n                    );\n                }\n                Header::None => {}\n            }\n        }\n        first = false;"
}

> AGENT

Done. Default is now `=== . ===` / `=== .claude ===`.

> DEVELOPER

nice -s - and works and not errors about bad flags, but using one or more asterisk must be quoted, oh well, maybe mention that in README. Another other characters require quoting?

> AGENT

Shell metacharacters that need quoting in `-s` values:

- `*` — glob expansion
- `?` — glob expansion
- `!` — history expansion (bash)
- `#` — comment
- `$` — variable expansion
- `` ` `` — command substitution
- `(` `)` `{` `}` — subshell/brace expansion
- `|` `&` `;` — pipeline/control
- `<` `>` — redirection
- `~` — home expansion (at start)

In practice, the likely ones someone would actually use as decoration: `*`, `#`, `!`. Things like `---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`, `+++`, `~~~` all work unquoted.

I'll add a note to the README.

> AGENT

Done.

> DEVELOPER

Wow never new about "!!", LSNED

> AGENT

Yeah, `!!` repeats the last command — bash history expansion. It's actually useful sometimes:

```
sudo !!    # re-run last command as root
```

But it's a notorious gotcha inside double quotes, since bash expands it before the command even sees it. Single quotes are the safe zone for literal strings.

Ready to commit whenever you are.

> DEVELOPER

I fixed the default typo in Multi-repo queries. Another observation, we chose "s" for seperator but it's a header obviously we can't use h, I'm fine with s but maybe there is a better name?

> AGENT

The flag letter `-s` is fine — it's mnemonic enough and short flags don't need to be perfect. For the long name though, `--separator` is a bit misleading now. Maybe `--header` for the long form?

- `-s "---"` / `--header "---"` — custom decoration
- `-S` / `--no-header` — suppress

That way the help text reads naturally. Or keep `--separator` since that's what users might intuitively reach for? Up to you.

> DEVELOPER

How about --label -l? Also, let's add sample output as it's not obvious what it's labeling?

> AGENT

`-l` is already taken by `--limit` (alias for `-n`). So `--label` with a different short... but we're running low on intuitive single letters.

Options:
- Keep `-s` short, rename long to `--label` / `--no-label` (`-S`)
- Use no short for label, just `--label` / `--no-label`

I'd say keep `-s`/`-S` as the short forms and rename the longs to `--label`/`--no-label`. Sound good?

And yes, I'll add sample output to the README showing what the headers look like.

> DEVELOPER

limit is vistigual and is an alias so we can use -l & --limit I think

> AGENT

Makes sense — repurpose `-l`/`--label` and `-L`/`--no-label`, drop the vestigial `limit` alias from `--commits`. Let me update all four subcommands.

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→
     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
     5→
     6→use crate::common;
     7→
     8→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
     9→pub struct ChidArgs {
    10→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    11→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    12→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    13→
    14→    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
    15→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    16→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    17→
    18→    /// Revision to look up (default: @)
    19→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    20→    pub revision: String,
    21→
    22→    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated (default: .)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
    24→    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
    25→
    26→    /// Number of changeIDs to show
    27→    #[arg(
    28→        short = 'n',
    29→        long = "commits",
    30→        alias = "limit",
    31→        short_alias = 'l',
    32→        value_name = "COMMITS"
    33→    )]
    34→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    35→
    36→    /// Custom header decoration between repos (default: ===)
    37→    #[arg(
    38→        short = 's',
    39→        long = "separator",
    40→        value_name = "TEXT",
    41→        allow_hyphen_values = true
    42→    )]
    43→    pub separator: Option<String>,
    44→
    45→    /// Suppress header between repos
    46→    #[arg(short = 'S', long = "no-separator")]
    47→    pub no_separator: bool,
    48→}
    49→
    50→pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    51→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
    52→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    53→        args.pos_count,
    54→        &args.revision,
    55→        args.limit,
    56→        "@",
    57→    );
    58→    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&args.separator, args.no_separator);
    59→
    60→    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {
    61→        let (ids, anchor_index) =
    62→            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
    63→
    64→        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
    65→            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    66→            let line = common::format_chid(&commit);
    67→            if i == anchor_index {
    68→                println!("{}", common::bold(&line));
    69→            } else {
    70→                println!("{line}");
    71→            }
    72→        }
    73→        Ok(())
    74→    })
    75→}
    76→

  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn chid_label() {\n        let cli = parse(&[\"vc-x1\", \"chid\", \"-l\", \"===\"]);\n        if let Commands::Chid(args) = cli.command {\n            assert_eq!(args.label, Some(\"===\".to_string()));\n            assert!(!args.no_label);\n        } else {\n            panic!(\"expected Chid\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn chid_no_label() {\n        let cli = parse(&[\"vc-x1\", \"chid\", \"-L\"]);\n        if let Commands::Chid(args) = cli.command {\n            assert!(args.no_label);\n            assert_eq!(args.label, None);\n        } else {\n            panic!(\"expected Chid\");\n        }"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/README.md",
  "old_string": "Control the header between repos with `-s` and `-S`:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default: === . ===  /  === .claude ===\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -s \"---\"   # custom:  --- . ---  /  --- .claude ---\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -S         # no header (raw output)\n```\n\nMost decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,\n`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters\nlike `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-s '***'`). Double quotes\nwon't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).",
  "new_string": "Control the label between repos with `-l` and `-L`:\n\n```\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default: === . ===  /  === .claude ===\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l \"---\"   # custom:  --- . ---  /  --- .claude ---\nvc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)\n```\n\nSample output with default label:\n\n```\n=== . ===\nkwoyvposvsmv\n\n=== .claude ===\nzzpksklxyrnw\n```\n\nMost decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,\n`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters\nlike `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes\nwon't protect against `!` (bash history expansion)."
}

```
     1→# Memory
     2→
     3→## User Preferences
     4→
     5→- **Commit approval**: Always present the full commit commands to the user for physical review BEFORE executing them. Do not run jj commit / git commit until the user explicitly approves.
     6→- **Versioning**: Every plan must start with a version bump per notes/README.md "Versioning during development". Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step approach before starting.
     7→- **Pre-commit checklist**: Before committing, run: tests, clippy, fmt, install the app, then retest.
     8→
     9→## Feedback
    10→
    11→- [Always include ochid trailer in commits](feedback_ochid_trailer.md)
    12→- [Always commit .claude repo before finalize](feedback_claude_commit_before_finalize.md)
    13→- [Skip .claude push before finalize — finalize handles it](feedback_no_claude_push.md)
    14→- [Finalize must be absolute last action — only status line after](feedback_finalize_last.md)
    15→- [Use --ignore-immutable for late squash after push](feedback_squash_ignore_immutable.md)
    16→- [Use vc-x1 chid to retrieve changeIDs, not jj log](feedback_use_chid.md)
    17→- [Recognize when stuck — 5+ min thinking on simple task means stuck](feedback_stuck_detection.md)
    18→- [No --allow-new flag for jj git push](feedback_jj_no_allow_new.md)
    19→- [No text output after finalize — trailing writes miss squash window](feedback_no_finalize_echo.md)
    20→- [Ask for clarification on ambiguous input instead of spinning](feedback_ask_on_ambiguity.md)
    21→- ["finalize steps" triggers full session-end workflow](feedback_finalize_steps.md)
    22→
    23→## Lessons Learned
    24→
    25→- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
    26→- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
    27→- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
    28→
    29→## Key Files
    30→
    31→- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
    32→- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
    33→- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
    34→- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
    35→
```

```
    87→### Multi-repo queries
    88→
    89→The `-R`/`--repo` flag can be repeated or comma-separated to query
    90→multiple repos at once. When multiple repos are given, output is
    91→labeled with bold `=== path ===` headers by default:
    92→
    93→```
    94→vc-x1 chid -R . -R .claude      # repeated flag
    95→vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude         # comma-separated
    96→vc-x1 list @.. 3 -R .,.claude   # works with all read-only subcommands
    97→```
    98→
    99→Control the label between repos with `-l` and `-L`:
   100→
   101→```
   102→vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude            # default: === . ===  /  === .claude ===
   103→vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -l "---"   # custom:  --- . ---  /  --- .claude ---
   104→vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L         # no label (raw output)
   105→```
   106→
   107→Sample output with default label:
   108→
   109→```
   110→=== . ===
   111→kwoyvposvsmv
   112→
   113→=== .claude ===
   114→zzpksklxyrnw
   115→```
   116→
   117→Most decoration strings work unquoted (`---`, `===`, `>>>`, `:::`,
   118→`+++`). Use single quotes for strings containing shell metacharacters
   119→like `*`, `!`, `#`, `$`, or `~` (e.g. `-l '***'`). Double quotes
   120→won't protect against `!` (bash history expansion).
   121→
   122→With a single repo (or no `-R`), no separator is printed — backward
   123→compatible with previous behavior. Multi-repo is supported for
   124→`chid`, `desc`, `list`, and `show`. `finalize` remains single-repo.
   125→
   126→### finalize
```

> DEVELOPER

You've put most tests in main.rs, not bad but makes it rather long and I feel moving them to subcommand files and/or tests/ might be better?

> AGENT

Yeah, `main.rs` is ~600 lines with tests dominating. The CLI parsing tests could live in each subcommand's file since they're testing that module's args struct. The pattern would be:

- `chid.rs` — chid args tests
- `desc.rs` — desc args tests  
- `list.rs` — list args tests
- `show.rs` — show args tests
- `finalize.rs` — finalize args tests (it already has one)
- `main.rs` — just `unknown_command` and any cross-cutting tests

Want to tackle that as the next task, or save it for later? I noticed you already added it to the todo.

> DEVELOPER

thoughts on integration tests in tests/

> AGENT

Integration tests in `tests/` would be good for testing actual command output — running `vc-x1 chid`, `vc-x1 list`, etc. against real temp jj repos and asserting on stdout. That's different from the CLI parsing tests which just verify clap arg structures and don't need a repo.

I'd split it as:

- **Unit tests** (in each `src/*.rs`): CLI parsing, formatting, spec resolution — what we have now, just dispersed
- **Integration tests** (`tests/`): spawn the binary against temp jj repos, assert actual output — covers the `finalize` todo item and could cover multi-repo too

The two efforts are independent. Want to do the test dispersal first (quick cleanup), or jump to integration tests (more involved, needs `tempfile` + `assert_cmd` or similar)?

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→
     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
     5→
     6→use crate::common;
     7→
     8→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
     9→pub struct ChidArgs {
    10→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    11→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    12→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    13→
    14→    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
    15→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    16→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    17→
    18→    /// Revision to look up (default: @)
    19→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    20→    pub revision: String,
    21→
    22→    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated (default: .)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
    24→    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
    25→
    26→    /// Number of changeIDs to show
    27→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
    28→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    29→
    30→    /// Custom label decoration between repos (default: ===)
    31→    #[arg(
    32→        short = 'l',
    33→        long = "label",
    34→        value_name = "TEXT",
    35→        allow_hyphen_values = true
    36→    )]
    37→    pub label: Option<String>,
    38→
    39→    /// Suppress label between repos
    40→    #[arg(short = 'L', long = "no-label")]
    41→    pub no_label: bool,
    42→}
    43→
    44→pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    45→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
    46→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    47→        args.pos_count,
    48→        &args.revision,
    49→        args.limit,
    50→        "@",
    51→    );
    52→    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&args.label, args.no_label);
    53→
    54→    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {
    55→        let (ids, anchor_index) =
    56→            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
    57→
    58→        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
    59→            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    60→            let line = common::format_chid(&commit);
    61→            if i == anchor_index {
    62→                println!("{}", common::bold(&line));
    63→            } else {
    64→                println!("{line}");
    65→            }
    66→        }
    67→        Ok(())
    68→    })
    69→}
    70→
```

```
     1→# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions for hw-jjg-bot
     2→
     3→## Project Structure
     4→
     5→This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
     6→
     7→1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
     8→2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
     9→
    10→Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
    11→
    12→## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
    13→
    14→- App repo: `.` (project root)
    15→- Bot session repo: `.claude`
    16→  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
    17→
    18→## Committing
    19→
    20→Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
    21→relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
    22→visible at the start of the command.
    23→
    24→### App repo
    25→```
    26→jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .
    27→```
    28→
    29→### Bot session repo
    30→```
    31→jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .claude
    32→```
    33→
    34→## jj Basics
    35→
    36→- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
    37→- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
    38→- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
    39→- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
    40→- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
    41→  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
    42→- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
    43→  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
    44→- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
    45→  session because session data updates continuously.
    46→
    47→## Pre-commit Requirements
    48→
    49→### User approval
    50→
    51→Never execute commit commands without the user's explicit approval.
    52→Present the full commands for review first; only run them after the
    53→user confirms.
    54→
    55→### Notes references
    56→
    57→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    58→See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
    59→
    60→### Versioning
    61→
    62→Every change must start with a version bump. See
    63→[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
    64→for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
    65→
    66→### Pre-commit checklist
    67→
    68→Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
    69→
    70→1. `cargo fmt`
    71→2. `cargo clippy`
    72→3. `cargo test`
    73→4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
    74→5. Retest after install
    75→
    76→## Session End Workflows
    77→
    78→When the user asks to "commit both repos" or says they're done, commit
    79→both repos. Use the **same title** for both commits so they're easy to
    80→correlate. The body can differ: the app repo body should summarize code
    81→changes; the bot session repo body should note what was done in the
    82→session.
    83→
    84→```
    85→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
    86→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
    87→```
    88→
    89→After both commits, **pause and ask for user approval** before
    90→proceeding to bookmark/push/finalize.
    91→
    92→When the user also asks to push, advance the current bookmark on both
    93→repos, then push the app repo. Do **not** push `.claude` here —
    94→`finalize` handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
    95→
    96→Replace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,
    97→`dev-0.14.0`).
    98→
    99→```
   100→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
   101→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude
   102→jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
   103→```
   104→
   105→### Late changes after push
   106→
   107→If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
   108→updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
   109→`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
   110→
   111→```
   112→jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
   113→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
   114→jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
   115→```
   116→
   117→### Finalize the .claude repo
   118→
   119→The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
   120→This squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The
   121→delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
   122→short relative path for `--repo`.
   123→
   124→**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
   125→calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
   126→finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
   127→
   128→`--bookmark` is required — use the active bookmark for the session.
   129→
   130→```
   131→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --push
   132→```
   133→
   134→Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
   135→already displays it. Any trailing text output creates writes that
   136→miss the finalize squash window.
   137→
```

   157→/// Format: just the short changeID.
   158→pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   159→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   160→    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
   161→}
   162→
   163→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line (single line).
   164→pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   165→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   166→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   167→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   168→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   169→    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   170→    if first_line.is_empty() {
   171→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   172→    } else {
   173→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
   174→    }
   175→}
   176→
   177→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.
   178→pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   179→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   180→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   181→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   182→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   183→    let desc = commit.description();
   184→    if desc.is_empty() {
   185→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   186→    } else {
   187→        let mut lines = desc.lines();
   188→        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("");
   189→        let mut result = format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}");
   190→        for line in lines {
   191→            result.push('\n');
   192→            result.push_str(line);
   193→        }
   194→        result
   195→    }
   196→}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Format: just the short changeID.",
  "new_string": "/// Extract the ochid trailer value from a commit description, if present.\npub fn extract_ochid(commit: &Commit) -> Option<String> {\n    for line in commit.description().lines().rev() {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"ochid:\") {\n            return Some(value.trim().to_string());\n        }\n    }\n    None\n}\n\n/// Format: changeID ochid(padded) first-line (single line).\n///\n/// ochid column is left-padded to `width` characters. If no ochid, a blank\n/// placeholder of that width is used.\npub fn format_commit_with_ochid(commit: &Commit, width: usize) -> String {\n    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n    let ochid = extract_ochid(commit).unwrap_or_default();\n    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n    let title = if first_line.is_empty() {\n        \"(no description set)\"\n    } else {\n        first_line\n    };\n    format!(\"{change_short} {ochid:<width$} {title}\")\n}\n\n/// Format: just the short changeID."
}
```

    14→    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)

    43→
    44→pub fn list(args: &ListArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    45→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
    46→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    47→        args.pos_count,
    48→        &args.revision,
    49→        args.limit,
    50→        "@",
    51→    );

    53→
    54→    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {

    56→            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
    57→

    63→            } else {

    65→            }
    66→        }
    67→        Ok(())

  "new_string": "    /// Suppress label between repos\n    #[arg(short = 'L', long = \"no-label\")]\n    pub no_label: bool,\n\n    /// ochid column width (default: 20)\n    #[arg(short = 'w', long = \"width\", default_value_t = 20)]\n    pub width: usize,\n}\n\npub fn list(args: &ListArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let spec = common::resolve_spec(\n        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n        args.pos_count,\n        &args.revision,\n        args.limit,\n        \"@\",\n    );\n    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&args.label, args.no_label);\n\n    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {\n        let (ids, anchor_index) =\n            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;\n\n        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n            let line = common::format_commit_with_ochid(&commit, args.width);\n            if i == anchor_index {\n                println!(\"{}\", common::bold(&line));\n            } else {\n                println!(\"{line}\");\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    })\n}"
}

> AGENT

Good catch — the ochid values should use the same 12-char short changeID that `vc-x1 chid` outputs. The older commits had full-length IDs in the trailer. When we redo them, we'll normalize to 12 chars.

For the `extract_ochid` function, it just returns whatever's in the trailer as-is, so the column width variation comes from the data, not the code. The `-w 20` default accommodates `/.claude/` (9 chars) + 12-char ID (21 total) with slight push — might want to bump default to 22 so the common case fits without overflow?

Or keep 20 and accept the 1-char push for `/.claude/` paths?

For `-R` and `-l` the manual "(default: ...)" is the only way to communicate the default, so those should stay. Let me fix just the duplicates.

> DEVELOPER

Maybe odd, but why not make a default_value = "==="` for options like `--label`

> AGENT

Good point — if we give `-l` a `default_value = "==="`, clap handles the default display automatically and we can simplify the code. The only reason I didn't was because `None` meant "use default header" vs `Some(s)` meant "custom" — but we can just always use the value and drop the `Header::Default` variant.

Wait, but then `-l` would always be `Some("===")` and we couldn't distinguish "user didn't pass -l" from "user passed -l ===". That matters for single-repo mode where we suppress the header entirely. Currently `for_each_repo` only shows headers when `multi && !None`.

Actually — the header is only shown when `repos.len() > 1` regardless. So the default value doesn't change single-repo behavior. We just always have a label string, and `-L` suppresses it. The `Header` enum simplifies to just `Custom(String)` or `None`.

Let me do it.

  "new_string": "/// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum Header {\n    /// Show `deco path deco` header per repo.\n    Label(String),\n    /// No header at all.\n    None,\n}\n\n/// Resolve `-l` / `-L` flags into a `Header`.\n///\n/// `-L` (no header) takes precedence over `-l` (label decoration).\npub fn resolve_header(label: &str, suppress: bool) -> Header {\n    if suppress {\n        Header::None\n    } else {\n        Header::Label(label.to_string())\n    }\n}"
}

String: /// Header style between repos in multi-repo output.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum Header {
    /// Bold `=== path ===` header per repo (default).
    Default,
    /// Custom decoration: `deco path deco`.
    Custom(String),
    /// No header at all.
    None,
}

/// Resolve `-s` / `-S` flags into a `Header`.
///
/// `-S` (no header) takes precedence over `-s` (custom decoration).
pub fn resolve_header(custom: &Option<String>, suppress: bool) -> Header {
    if suppress {
        Header::None
    } else if let Some(s) = custom {
        Header::Custom(s.clone())
    } else {
        Header::Default
    }
}</tool_use_error>

> AGENT

Yes — clap supports `#[command(flatten)]` which lets you define a shared args struct and embed it in each subcommand. Something like:

```rust
// common.rs
#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct RepoArgs {
    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
    
    #[arg(short = 'l', long = "label", ..., default_value = "===")]
    pub label: String,
    
    #[arg(short = 'L', long = "no-label")]
    pub no_label: bool,
}

// chid.rs
pub struct ChidArgs {
    #[command(flatten)]
    pub repo: RepoArgs,
    
    // chid-specific args...
}
```

That would deduplicate `-R`, `-l`, `-L`, `-r`, `-n`, and the positional args too. Good cleanup but separate from 0.21.0 — add it as a todo?

     1→# Chores-02
     2→
     3→## Graphical tools for jj repos
     4→
     5→### GG (gg)
     6→
     7→Cross-platform GUI for jj (Tauri-based). Interactive graph view.
     8→
     9→- Install: https://github.com/gulbanana/gg
    10→- `gg gui` does NOT work; use `gg web` instead (opens in browser)
    11→
    12→### jj-fzf
    13→
    14→fzf-based TUI centered on `jj log` graph. Previews diffs, evolog,
    15→op log. Many keybindings for common jj operations.
    16→
    17→- Install: https://github.com/tim-janik/jj-fzf
    18→- Requires `fzf` installed separately (`sudo pacman -S fzf` on Arch)
    19→
    20→### Others (not tested)
    21→
    22→- **jjui** — TUI for jj (https://github.com/idursun/jjui)
    23→- **jjgui** — commercial desktop app (https://jj-gui.com/)
    24→- **Jujutsu Kaizen** — VS Code plugin
    25→- **VisualJJ** — VS Code plugin (native jj, no git colocation)
    26→- **Selvejj** — JetBrains plugin
    27→
    28→### Built-in
    29→
    30→- `jj log` — shows graph by default (no `--graph` flag needed)
    31→- `gitk --all` — works on the colocated `.git` but sees raw git refs,
    32→  not jj's view of history
    33→
    34→## jj commit organization and traversal mechanisms (0.17.0)
    35→
    36→### Motivation
    37→
    38→Understanding how jj commits are organized and what jj-lib APIs exist
    39→for traversing them — a prerequisite for building richer views of
    40→codebase evolution over time. The todo item "determine the organization
    41→of commits in jj and how we can iterate over them" prompted this
    42→investigation.
    43→
    44→### Three axes of traversal
    45→
    46→jj provides three distinct ways to navigate commit history:
    47→
    48→1. **Commit DAG** — parent/child relationships between commits
    49→2. **Refs/bookmarks** — named pointers (bookmarks, tags, git refs) into the DAG
    50→3. **Evolution log (evolog)** — rewrite history of a single ChangeId across operations
    51→
    52→Each answers a different question:
    53→- DAG: "what is the commit history?"
    54→- Refs: "where are the named branches pointing?"
    55→- Evolog: "how was this particular change built up?"
    56→
    57→### Commit DAG traversal via revsets
    58→
    59→jj-lib's revset engine supports both string-based and programmatic queries.
    60→
    61→**String-based** (what vc-x1 uses today via `common::resolve_revset`):
    62→```rust
    63→resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, "::@")?  // ancestors of @
    64→resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, "all()")? // all visible commits
    65→```
    66→
    67→**Programmatic** (builder API, no parsing needed):
    68→```rust
    69→use jj_lib::revset::RevsetExpression;
    70→
    71→RevsetExpression::all()                    // all visible + referenced
    72→RevsetExpression::visible_heads()          // tips of visible branches
    73→RevsetExpression::symbol("@".into())       // working copy
    74→RevsetExpression::commits(vec![id])        // explicit set
    75→RevsetExpression::bookmarks(pattern)       // bookmark refs
    76→RevsetExpression::git_refs()               // all git refs
    77→
    78→// Chainable traversal
    79→expr.ancestors()           // ::x
    80→expr.descendants()         // x::
    81→expr.parents()             // direct parents
    82→expr.children()            // direct children
    83→expr.heads()               // maximal elements
    84→expr.roots()               // minimal elements
    85→expr.fork_point()          // merge base
    86→
    87→// Ranges and set operations
    88→expr1.range(&expr2)        // x..y
    89→expr1.dag_range_to(&expr2) // x::y
    90→expr1.union(&expr2)
    91→expr1.intersection(&expr2)
    92→expr1.minus(&expr2)
    93→```
    94→
    95→### Iteration modes
    96→
    97→Evaluated revsets support three iteration modes:
    98→
    99→```rust
   100→let revset = expr.evaluate(repo)?;
   101→
   102→revset.iter()               // yields CommitId in topo order (newest first)
   103→revset.commit_change_ids()  // yields (CommitId, ChangeId) pairs
   104→revset.iter_graph()         // yields GraphNode<CommitId> with edge info
   105→```
   106→
   107→`iter_graph()` provides `GraphEdge` data with edge types (`Direct`,
   108→`Indirect`, `Missing`) — needed for rendering graph views like
   109→`jj log --graph`.
   110→
   111→### Ref/bookmark access via View
   112→
   113→```rust
   114→let view = repo.view();
   115→view.heads()              // HashSet<CommitId> — visible branch tips
   116→view.bookmarks()          // all bookmarks with local+remote state
   117→view.local_bookmarks()    // local bookmarks only
   118→view.git_refs()           // BTreeMap of git refs
   119→view.git_head()           // git HEAD
   120→```
   121→
   122→### Index (direct DAG queries)
   123→
   124→```rust
   125→let index = repo.index();
   126→index.is_ancestor(&id1, &id2)?           // ancestry check
   127→index.common_ancestors(&[id1], &[id2])?  // merge base
   128→index.heads(&mut candidates)?            // compute heads
   129→index.all_heads_for_gc()?                // every head including hidden
   130→```
   131→
   132→### Low-level DAG walks (dag_walk module)
   133→
   134→Generic traversal functions on arbitrary DAGs:
   135→
   136→```rust
   137→jj_lib::dag_walk::dfs(start, id_fn, neighbors_fn)
   138→jj_lib::dag_walk::topo_order_forward(...)
   139→jj_lib::dag_walk::topo_order_reverse(...)
   140→jj_lib::dag_walk::heads(...)
   141→jj_lib::dag_walk::closest_common_node(...)
   142→```
   143→
   144→### Evolution log (evolog)
   145→
   146→The evolog shows every version a ChangeId went through — snapshots,
   147→squashes, rebases, describes. It's tracked at the **operation level**,
   148→not in the commit DAG.
   149→
   150→Example output from `jj evolog -r @-`:
   151→```
   152→◆    rpyrwwnw wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:53:12 main f7f567f6
   153→├─╮  Finalize: replace --foreground with --detach, ...
   154→│ │  -- operation 655ff50a3386 squash commits into ...
   155→│ ○  tluyvppo/0 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:53:12 89b1c104 (hidden)
   156→│ │  (no description set)
   157→│ │  -- operation e71fa874e37a snapshot working copy
   158→│ ○  tluyvppo/1 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:49:54 317cefcf (hidden)
   159→│    (empty) (no description set)
   160→│    -- operation e1fe385617e5 commit ...
   161→○  rpyrwwnw/1 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:49:54 50c30496 (hidden)
   162→│  Finalize: replace --foreground with --detach, ...
   163→...
   164→```
   165→
   166→**Core API** (`jj_lib::evolution`):
   167→
   168→```rust
   169→use jj_lib::evolution::{walk_predecessors, CommitEvolutionEntry};
   170→
   171→// Walk the rewrite history of a commit
   172→let iter = walk_predecessors(repo, &[commit_id]);
   173→for entry in iter {
   174→    let entry: CommitEvolutionEntry = entry?;
   175→    // entry.commit        — the commit at this point in history
   176→    // entry.operation      — the operation that created this version
   177→    // entry.predecessor_ids() — IDs of the previous version(s)
   178→    // entry.predecessors()    — iterator over predecessor Commit objects
   179→}
   180→
   181→// Batch resolve predecessors across operation ranges
   182→let map: BTreeMap<CommitId, Vec<CommitId>> =
   183→    accumulate_predecessors(new_ops, old_ops)?;
   184→```
   185→
   186→### Evolog data is local-only
   187→
   188→**Critical finding**: operation/evolution data does NOT survive a git
   189→push + clone cycle. This is by design.
   190→
   191→From `op_store.rs`:
   192→> "Operations and views are not meant to be exchanged between repos or
   193→> users; they represent local state and history."
   194→
   195→| Data                  | Pushed? | Recoverable on clone? |
   196→|-----------------------|---------|----------------------|
   197→| Git commits + trees   | Yes     | Yes                  |
   198→| Change IDs            | No      | Yes (from git header)|
   199→| Commit predecessors   | No      | No                   |
   200→| Operation history     | No      | No                   |
   201→| Evolution log         | No      | No                   |
   202→
   203→Predecessor tracking is stored per-operation in
   204→`op_store::Operation::commit_predecessors` (since jj 0.30). The old
   205→`backend::Commit::predecessors` field is deprecated and slated for
   206→removal around jj 0.42.
   207→
   208→### Implications for codebase evolution story
   209→
   210→The evolog provides a rich local narrative of how changes were built
   211→up — intermediate snapshots, false starts, squashes — that commit
   212→messages alone don't capture. However, this data is ephemeral across
   213→git transport.
   214→
   215→For the dual-repo workflow (app + `.claude`), the `.claude` repo's
   216→evolog captures how session commits were constructed. If `.claude`
   217→eventually becomes a git submodule or is backed by a database, the
   218→evolog data could be extracted and persisted via `walk_predecessors()`
   219→before it's lost to a clone. This would preserve the full provenance
   220→chain: not just what changed, but how the change was assembled.
   221→
   222→## Interactive DAG navigation: the missing feature (2026-03-18)
   223→
   224→### The problem
   225→
   226→When working with jj-git repos, especially complex ones, you need to
   227→navigate the commit DAG interactively — click a parent or child and
   228→jump to it instantly. This is table-stakes in gitk but absent from
   229→jj-native tooling.
   230→
   231→### gitk: still the best for DAG traversal
   232→
   233→gitk's killer feature: the parent/child list lets you **click-to-jump**
   234→to any commit, even if it's far off-screen. True graph traversal, not
   235→scrolling. Works in colocated jj repos via `jj git export`.
   236→
   237→**Downsides:** Shows Git's view only — no change IDs, no revsets, no
   238→jj bookmarks. Detached HEAD display is confusing. Cannot perform jj
   239→operations. Does not work with non-colocated jj repos.
   240→
   241→### gg-cli: close but not there
   242→
   243→Renders a true graphical DAG. Can show parents in the detail pane and
   244→click to select a parent — but **only if that parent is already visible
   245→in the left panel**. If the parent is off-screen, there is no jump-to
   246→behavior; you must manually scroll using the slider. This makes it
   247→**useless on large/complex repos** for DAG traversal.
   248→
   249→Drag-and-drop rebase is nice, but the lack of click-to-jump is a
   250→dealbreaker for navigation.
   251→
   252→### Other tools: list navigators, not graph navigators
   253→
   254→- **jj-fzf** — displays `jj log` graph in fzf with live revset editing.
   255→  Navigation is list-based (up/down), not graph-based (follow edges).
   256→- **jjui** — revision tree view, growing fast, but still fundamentally
   257→  a list navigator with a graph drawn alongside.
   258→- **lazyjj** — operations-focused TUI, not DAG-focused.
   259→- **jjdag** — pre-alpha, explicitly DAG-focused (Magit-style keys,
   260→  foldable tree). Worth watching.
   261→- **jj log** — revset language is powerful for querying (`children(x)`,
   262→  `ancestors(main..@)`) but output is static, not interactive.
   263→
   264→### Comparison
   265→
   266→| Tool     | DAG display | Click-to-jump parent/child | Native jj |
   267→|----------|-------------|---------------------------|-----------|
   268→| gitk     | graphical   | **yes** (both directions)  | no        |
   269→| gg-cli   | graphical   | parent only, no jump if off-screen | yes |
   270→| jj-fzf   | ASCII       | no                         | yes       |
   271→| jjui     | ASCII       | no                         | yes       |
   272→| jj log   | ASCII       | n/a (not interactive)      | yes       |
   273→
   274→### Conclusion
   275→
   276→**gitk remains the best tool for interactive DAG traversal** despite
   277→its jj blindspots. The jj ecosystem is missing a tool that combines
   278→jj-native concepts (change IDs, revsets, bookmarks) with gitk-style
   279→click-to-jump on parent/child links. This would be a strong feature
   280→request for gg-cli, which already has the infrastructure but lacks the
   281→jump-to behavior.
   282→
   283→### Cautionary tale: multi-tool dangers
   284→
   285→While testing jj-fzf and gg-cli simultaneously (with Zed editor also
   286→open), it's easy to accidentally move `@` to an unexpected commit via
   287→one tool without noticing. When Zed then sees files disappear from the
   288→working copy, closing a "changed" file and declining to save effectively
   289→deletes it. Recovery: `jj new main` to repoint `@`, or
   290→`jj file show <path> -r <rev> --at-op <op>` to extract from history.
   291→
   292→## jj-lib Commit API and tree storage model (2026-03-18)
   293→
   294→### What's on the Commit type
   295→
   296→Given a `Commit` (from `repo.store().get_commit(&commit_id)?`), the
   297→public API includes:
   298→
   299→| Method | Returns | Cost |
   300→|--------|---------|------|
   301→| `id()` | `&CommitId` | zero — stored in struct |
   302→| `change_id()` | `&ChangeId` | zero |
   303→| `description()` | `&str` | zero |
   304→| `author()` / `committer()` | `&Signature` | zero |
   305→| `parent_ids()` | `&[CommitId]` | zero — stored in commit data |
   306→| `parents()` | `Vec<Commit>` | async, loads parent commits |
   307→| `tree()` | `MergedTree` | near-zero — wraps tree IDs, no I/O |
   308→| `tree_ids()` | `&Merge<TreeId>` | zero |
   309→| `parent_tree(repo)` | `MergedTree` | async, merges parent trees |
   310→| `is_empty(repo)` | `bool` | compares tree to parent tree |
   311→| `has_conflict()` | `bool` | checks if tree IDs are conflicted |
   312→
   313→### What's NOT on Commit
   314→
   315→- **Children** — parent→child is a reverse lookup. Requires revsets:
   316→  ```rust
   317→  resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &format!("children({})", change_id))?
   318→  ```
   319→  Parents are stored in the commit; children require scanning the DAG.
   320→
   321→- **Diff/changes** — no method returns "what changed in this commit."
   322→  Must be computed by comparing two tree snapshots.
   323→
   324→### Getting diffs between a commit and its parent
   325→
   326→```rust
   327→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
   328→
   329→let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo).await?;
   330→let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   331→
   332→let mut diff_stream = parent_tree.diff_stream(&commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   333→while let Some(entry) = diff_stream.next().await {
   334→    // entry.path  — RepoPathBuf (the file that changed)
   335→    // entry.values — Diff { before, after } of MergedTreeValue
   336→}
   337→```
   338→
   339→`parent_tree()` handles merge commits by merging multiple parent trees.
   340→
   341→### What's stored on disk
   342→
   343→jj uses git as its backend. On disk: git's content-addressable objects.
   344→
   345→- **Blobs** — file contents, deduplicated by SHA
   346→- **Trees** — directory listings (pointers to blobs and sub-trees)
   347→- **Commits** — pointer to root tree + parent IDs + metadata
   348→
   349→Each commit points to a **full tree snapshot**, but the tree is composed
   350→of shared objects. If a file didn't change, both commits' trees point to
   351→the same blob. Git also delta-compresses objects in packfiles.
   352→
   353→The "full snapshot" is **logical, not physical**. `commit.tree()` just
   354→wraps the root tree hash — no I/O happens until you traverse.
   355→
   356→### Cost model for tree operations
   357→
   358→| Operation | Cost | Why |
   359→|-----------|------|-----|
   360→| `commit.tree()` | near zero | wraps tree IDs, no I/O |
   361→| `tree.path_value(&path)` | O(path depth) | walks ~4-5 tree nodes |
   362→| `tree.diff_stream(&other)` | O(changed subtrees) | skips identical subtrees by hash |
   363→| `tree.entries()` | O(entire repo) | walks everything — avoid on large repos |
   364→
   365→The diff algorithm compares tree entries level by level. If a subtree
   366→hash matches, it's skipped entirely — never descended into. On a Linux
   367→monorepo commit that touches 5 files, it loads ~20-30 tree objects out
   368→of millions.
   369→
   370→The expensive part of a gitk-style patch view is reading **blob
   371→contents** for the line-by-line diff, but only for changed files.
   372→
   373→### jj-lib vs gitk efficiency
   374→
   375→jj-lib is a separate Rust implementation reading the same `.git/objects/`
   376→and packfiles — it does NOT shell out to git or link libgit2.
   377→
   378→The tree diff uses the **same hash-based short-circuiting** as git:
   379→`TreeDiffIterator` (in `merged_tree.rs`) checks `is_changed()` on
   380→`MergedTreeValue` (which compares tree hashes) and only recurses into
   381→directories that differ.
   382→
   383→jj-lib also has a feature gitk lacks: **concurrent tree loading**. When
   384→`store.concurrency() > 1`, it uses `TreeDiffStreamImpl` with async
   385→parallel I/O for loading tree objects, potentially faster than gitk on
   386→large repos.
   387→
   388→Source: `jj-lib-0.39.0/src/merged_tree.rs`, lines 260-690.
   389→
   390→### Key jj-lib source files (0.39.0)
   391→
   392→- `src/revset.rs` — revset expression types, builders, parsing
   393→- `src/evolution.rs` — evolog/predecessor traversal
   394→- `src/graph.rs` — graph structures, TopoGroupedGraphIterator
   395→- `src/dag_walk.rs` — generic DAG traversal (DFS, topo sort)
   396→- `src/repo.rs` — Repo trait, ReadonlyRepo
   397→- `src/view.rs` — View (heads, bookmarks, git refs)
   398→- `src/index.rs` — Index trait (ancestry, common ancestors)
   399→- `src/commit.rs` — Commit wrapper (parent_ids, change_id, description)
   400→- `src/backend.rs` — backend data types (CommitId, ChangeId, Signature)
   401→- `src/op_store.rs` — operation storage (commit_predecessors)
   402→- `src/operation.rs` — Operation type (predecessors_for_commit)
   403→
   404→## Show subcommand (0.18.0)
   405→
   406→### 0.18.0 — Initial show subcommand
   407→
   408→Implement `vc-x1 show` matching `jj show` output: commit/change IDs,
   409→bookmarks (local + remote), author/committer with timestamps,
   410→indented description, and file-level diff summary (Added/Modified/Removed).
   411→Uses `TreeDiffIterator` for sync tree diffing via jj-lib.
   412→
   413→### 0.18.1 — Flesh out show header
   414→
   415→Show header now has gitk-style fields: Ids, Author, Committer, Parent,
   416→Child, Branches (ancestry-based), Follows/Precedes (nearest tags),
   417→Description (body only). Supports `..` notation for multi-commit display
   418→with separators. File list: `-f` flag (default 50, `0`=none, `all`=unlimited)
   419→with first/last split when truncated. Remove `--indent` from desc,
   420→hardcode 4-space indent in both desc and show.
   421→
   422→### 0.19.0 — Unify `..` notation and CLI across all subcommands
   423→
   424→- Extract `collect_ids` in `common.rs` — returns `(Vec<CommitId>, usize)`,
   425→  ordered commit IDs and anchor index. Handles all `..` directions.
   426→- Extract `resolve_spec` — converts positional/flag args into `DotSpec`
   427→  (desc_count/anc_count) ready for `collect_ids`. Single code path for
   428→  all subcommands.
   429→- Replace `DotDirection` enum with `desc_count`/`anc_count` on `DotSpec`.
   430→- Remove `resolve_dot_args`, `ResolvedArgs`, `collect_both`/`collect_commit_lines`,
   431→  `show_both` — all replaced by `resolve_spec` + `collect_ids`.
   432→- All subcommands (chid, desc, list, show) now share one loop pattern.
   433→- `-r` flag supports `..` notation (e.g. `-r ..mpl -n 3`).
   434→- Rename `-l`/`--limit` to `-n`/`--commits` (`-l`/`--limit` kept as
   435→  hidden aliases). Positional `COMMITS` replaces `POS_COUNT`.
   436→- Count means total commits including anchor (e.g. `x.. 3` = x + 2
   437→  ancestors). Fixes off-by-one in descendants and bare-rev-with-count.
   438→- File list truncation: show first N files only. Skip truncation when
   439→  only 1 file would be omitted.
   440→- Raw jj revset syntax (`::`, `|`, `&`) no longer supported via `-r`;
   441→  use `..` notation instead.
   442→
```

    20→use pollster::FutureExt;
    21→
    22→/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
    23→///
    24→/// Counts are `Some(0)` for closed sides and `None` for open (dotted) sides.
    25→/// Open sides become unlimited unless a positional count is applied later.
    26→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    27→pub struct DotSpec {
    28→    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
    29→    pub rev: String,
    30→    /// Descendant count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
    31→    pub desc_count: Option<usize>,
    32→    /// Ancestor count: `None` = open (dotted), `Some(0)` = closed.
    33→    pub anc_count: Option<usize>,
    34→}

 Commands::List(a) => a,\n            _ => panic!(\"expected List\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn defaults() {\n        let args = parse(&[\"vc-x1\", \"list\"]);\n     41→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    42→pub struct ShowArgs {
    43→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    44→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    45→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    46→
    47→    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
    48→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    49→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    50→
    51→    /// Revision to show
    52→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    53→    pub revision: String,
    54→
    55→    /// Max changed files: number, 0 (none), or 'all'
    56→    #[arg(short = 'f', long = "files", default_value = "50")]
    57→    pub files: String,
    58→
    59→    /// Number of commits to show
    60→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
    61→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    62→
    63→    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated [default: .]
    64→    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
    65→    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
    66→
    67→    /// Custom label decoration between repos
    68→    #[arg(
    69→        short = 'l',
    70→        long = "label",
    71→        value_name = "TEXT",
    72→        allow_hyphen_values = true,
    73→        default_value = "==="
    74→    )]
    75→    pub label: String,
    76→
    77→    /// Suppress label between repos
    78→    #[arg(short = 'L', long = "no-label")]
    79→    pub no_label: bool,
    80→}
    81→
    82→pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    83→    let file_limit = FileLimit::parse(&args.files)?;
    84→
    85→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
    86→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    87→        args.pos_count,
    88→        &args.revision,
    89→        args.limit,
    90→        "@",
    91→    );
    92→    let hdr = common::resolve_header(&args.label, args.no_label);
    93→
    94→    common::for_each_repo(&args.repos, &hdr, |workspace, repo| {
    95→        let (ids, anchor_index) =
    96→            common::collect_ids(workspace, repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;
    97→
    98→        let mut first = true;
    99→        for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {
   100→            if !first {
   101→                println!("────────────────────────────────────────");
   102→            }
   103→            first = false;
   104→
   105→            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   106→            show_one_commit(&commit, workspace, repo, file_limit, i == anchor_index)?;
   107→        }
   108→        Ok(())
   109→    })
   110→}
   111→
   112→fn show_one_commit(
   113→    commit: &Commit,
   114→    workspace: &Workspace,
   115→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   116→    file_limit: FileLimit,
   117→    is_primary: bool,
   118→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   119→    // Author
   120→    let author = commit.author();
   121→    println!(
   122→        "Author:    {} <{}> ({})",
   123→        author.name,
   124→        author.email,
   125→        format_timestamp(&author.timestamp)
   126→    );
   127→
   128→    // Committer
   129→    let committer = commit.committer();
   130→    println!(
   131→        "Committer: {} <{}> ({})",
   132→        committer.name,
   133→        committer.email,
   134→        format_timestamp(&committer.timestamp)
   135→    );

   440→- Raw jj revset syntax (`::`, `|`, `&`) no longer supported via `-r`;
   441→  use `..` notation instead.
   442→
   443→## Test dispersal and ochid list column
   444→
   445→### 0.20.1 — Disperse CLI parsing tests
   446→
   447→Move all CLI parsing tests from main.rs into per-subcommand test modules
   448→(chid.rs, desc.rs, list.rs, show.rs, finalize.rs). main.rs retains only
   449→the `unknown_command` test. Make `Commands` enum `pub(crate)` so submodule
   450→tests can match on it. Each module has a local `parse()` helper that
   451→returns the typed args struct directly, eliminating the `if let` boilerplate.
   452→
   453→### 0.21.0 — Show ochid in list output
   454→
   455→Replace commitID with ochid trailer value in `list` output. New column
   456→format: `chid  ochid  title` with 2-space gaps between columns. The ochid
   457→column is padded to `-w`/`--width` (default 21, fits `/.claude/` + 12-char
   458→changeID). Commits without an ochid trailer show a blank placeholder to
   459→keep columns aligned. Add `extract_ochid()` and `format_commit_with_ochid()`
   460→to common.rs. Also clean up CLI help: remove duplicate manual defaults from
   461→doc comments (clap shows `[default: ...]` automatically), use `default_value`
   462→for `--label`, and simplify `Header` enum from three variants to two
   463→(`Label(String)` and `None`).
   464→

> AGENT

Here's my thinking:

**The problem**: Recent commits have ochid trailers that are either bare changeIDs (missing the `/.claude/` or `/` path prefix) or use non-standard length IDs. We need to fix them in both repos.

**Why a subcommand makes sense**: 
- `jj describe` can rewrite commit descriptions, but you need to construct the correct new description for each commit
- Doing this manually across many commits in two repos is error-prone
- A subcommand like `vc-x1 fix-ochid` could scan commits, detect malformed ochid trailers, and rewrite them with `jj describe`

**Rough design for a `fix-ochid` subcommand**:
1. Take a revision range (`-r @.. -n 10` or similar)
2. For each commit, extract the ochid trailer
3. Check if it's malformed (missing path prefix, wrong ID length)
4. Given a `--repo-path` arg (like `/.claude/` or `/`), construct the correct ochid
5. Rewrite via `jj describe` (shell out, since jj-lib's write APIs are complex)
6. `--dry-run` mode to preview changes

**Challenge**: The bot can't fix its own `.claude` repo commits mid-session (session data continuously writes). So either:
- You run it manually after the session
- Or the subcommand handles both repos in one pass, fixing the app repo first then `.claude`

**Alternative**: Skip the subcommand and just script it with `jj describe` directly — it's a one-time fix. But having the subcommand is useful for future validation too.

What's your preference — subcommand or one-time script?

> DEVELOPER

Good analysis, I vote subcommand. But there is one gotcha the titles for several commands need fixing to, so maybe this subcommand takes a --title option with that will be replaced at the same time. Optionally on the face of it there is only the first three that are really jacked since that was the original idea. Actually, leaving them for historical reasons is probably better. Thoughts?

> AGENT

So the ones needing fixes are 0.19.1, 0.20.0, and 0.20.1 — they have bare changeIDs without the `/.claude/` prefix. Everything from 0.19.0 and earlier, plus 0.21.0+ is correct.

And the `.claude` repo has the mirror problem — those same commits need `/` prefix instead of bare IDs.

So the scope is small — 3 commits in each repo. A `fix-ochid` subcommand with `--dry-run` that:
1. Scans a range for malformed ochid trailers (missing path prefix)
2. Adds the correct prefix (`/.claude/` or `/` based on which repo it's in)
3. Optionally fixes `--title` at the same time
4. Rewrites via `jj describe`

For detecting "which prefix to use" — could infer from the repo path (if it contains `.claude`, the ochid should point to `/`, otherwise `/.claude/`). Or just take it as an arg.

> AGENT

Good — finalize already errors on multiple repos since it uses `PathBuf` not `Vec<PathBuf>`. The `fix-ochid` subcommand would use the same single `--repo` pattern.

So the design:
- `vc-x1 fix-ochid -r <range> -R <repo> --other-path <path>` 
- `--other-path` is the prefix to add (e.g. `/.claude/` or `/`)
- Single repo only, error on multiple
- `--dry-run` to preview
- Optional `--title <new-title>` to fix title at same time
- Rewrites via `jj describe`

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/fix_ochid.rs",
  "content": "use std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::process::Command;\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;\nuse jj_lib::repo::Repo;\n\nuse crate::common;\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct FixOchidArgs {\n    /// Revision(s) to fix\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Path prefix for the other repo (e.g. /.claude/ or /)\n    #[arg(long = \"other-path\")]\n    pub other_path: String,\n\n    /// New title to replace the first line (optional)\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub title: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Preview changes without writing\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub dry_run: bool,\n\n    /// Number of commits to fix\n    #[arg(short = 'n', long = \"commits\", value_name = \"COMMITS\")]\n    pub limit: Option<usize>,\n}\n\n/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.\n///\n/// If the description has an `ochid:` line, ensure the value starts with `other_path`.\n/// If it already has a path prefix starting with `/`, replace it.\n/// If it's a bare changeID, prepend `other_path`.\n/// Normalizes the changeID to 12 characters.\nfn fix_ochid_in_description(desc: &str, other_path: &str, new_title: Option<&str>) -> String {\n    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();\n\n    // Fix title if requested\n    if let Some(title) = new_title {\n        if !lines.is_empty() {\n            lines[0] = title.to_string();\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Fix ochid trailer\n    for line in &mut lines {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"ochid:\") {\n            let value = value.trim();\n            // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)\n            let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {\n                &value[pos + 1..]\n            } else {\n                value\n            };\n            // Normalize to 12 chars\n            let short_id = &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(12)];\n            // Reconstruct with correct path\n            let prefix = if other_path.ends_with('/') {\n                other_path.to_string()\n            } else {\n                format!(\"{other_path}/\")\n            };\n            *line = format!(\"ochid: {prefix}{short_id}\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Preserve trailing newline if original had one\n    let mut result = lines.join(\"\\n\");\n    if desc.ends_with('\\n') {\n        result.push('\\n');\n    }\n    result\n}\n\npub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    let revset_str = if let Some(n) = args.limit {\n        format!(\"ancestors({}, {})\", args.revision, n)\n    } else {\n        args.revision.clone()\n    };\n\n    let ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &revset_str)?;\n    if ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commits found for revision '{}'\", args.revision).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n    let mut fixed = 0;\n    let mut skipped = 0;\n\n    for commit_id in &ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        let desc = commit.description();\n        let change_hex =\n            jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n\n        let new_desc = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, &args.other_path, args.title.as_deref());\n\n        if new_desc == desc {\n            skipped += 1;\n            if args.dry_run {\n                println!(\"skip {change_short}  (no change needed)\");\n            }\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        if args.dry_run {\n            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n            println!(\"fix  {change_short}  {first_line}\");\n            // Show the ochid line\n            for line in new_desc.lines() {\n                if line.trim().starts_with(\"ochid:\") {\n                    println!(\"     ochid: {}\", line.trim().strip_prefix(\"ochid:\").unwrap().trim());\n                }\n            }\n        } else {\n            let status = Command::new(\"jj\")\n                .arg(\"describe\")\n                .arg(\"-m\")\n                .arg(&new_desc)\n                .arg(\"-r\")\n                .arg(&commit_id.hex())\n                .arg(\"-R\")\n                .arg(&args.repo)\n                .arg(\"--ignore-immutable\")\n                .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())\n                .stderr(std::process::Stdio::inherit())\n                .status()?;\n            if !status.success() {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"jj describe failed for {} (exit {})\",\n                    change_short,\n                    status.code().unwrap_or(-1)\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n            println!(\"fixed {change_short}  {first_line}\");\n        }\n        fixed += 1;\n    }\n\n    println!(\n        \"\\n{} fixed, {} skipped (of {} total)\",\n        fixed,\n        skipped,\n        ids.len()\n    );\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fix_bare_id() {\n        let desc = \"Some title\\n\\nBody text.\\n\\nochid: tzupykyyvnrp\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /.claude/tzupykyyvnrp\"));\n        assert!(result.starts_with(\"Some title\\n\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fix_wrong_prefix() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: /wrong/tzupykyyvnrp\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /.claude/tzupykyyvnrp\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn already_correct() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: /.claude/tzupykyyvnrp\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", None);\n        assert_eq!(result, desc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn truncate_long_id() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: abcdefghijklmnop\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/\", None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /abcdefghijkl\"));\n        assert!(!result.contains(\"mnop\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fix_title_and_ochid() {\n        let desc = \"Old title\\n\\nochid: bare12345678\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", Some(\"New title\"));\n        assert!(result.starts_with(\"New title\\n\"));\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /.claude/bare12345678\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn no_ochid_no_change() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nNo trailer here.\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", None);\n        assert_eq!(result, desc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn other_path_without_trailing_slash() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: abcdefghijkl\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/\", None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /abcdefghijkl\"));\n    }\n}\n"
}

     1→mod chid;
     2→mod common;
     3→mod desc;
     4→mod finalize;
     5→mod list;
     6→mod show;
     7→
     8→use std::process::ExitCode;
     9→
    10→use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};
    11→
    12→#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
    13→#[command(version, about = "vc-x1: jj workspace tooling")]
    14→pub struct Cli {
    15→    #[command(subcommand)]
    16→    pub(crate) command: Commands,
    17→}
    18→
    19→#[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
    20→pub(crate) enum Commands {
    21→    /// Print the changeID for a revision
    22→    Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
    23→
    24→    /// Show full description of a commit
    25→    Desc(desc::DescArgs),
    26→
    27→    /// List commits in a jj repo
    28→    List(list::ListArgs),
    29→
    30→    /// Show commit details and diff summary
    31→    Show(show::ShowArgs),
    32→
    33→    /// Squash working copy into target commit
    34→    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),
    35→}
    36→
    37→fn main() -> ExitCode {
    38→    let cli = Cli::parse();
    39→
    40→    match cli.command {
    41→        Commands::Chid(args) => {
    42→            if let Err(e) = chid::chid(&args) {
    43→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    44→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    45→            }
    46→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    47→        }
    48→        Commands::Desc(args) => {
    49→            if let Err(e) = desc::desc(&args) {
    50→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    51→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    52→            }
    53→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    54→        }
    55→        Commands::List(args) => {
    56→            if let Err(e) = list::list(&args) {
    57→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    58→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    59→            }
    60→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    61→        }
    62→        Commands::Show(args) => {
    63→            if let Err(e) = show::show(&args) {
    64→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    65→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    66→            }
    67→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    68→        }
    69→        Commands::Finalize(args) => {
    70→            let opts = args.into_opts();
    71→            finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize entry");
    72→            match finalize::finalize(&opts) {
    73→                Ok(()) => {
    74→                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize exit ok");
    75→                    ExitCode::SUCCESS
    76→                }
    77→                Err(e) => {
    78→                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("main: finalize exit err={e}"));
    79→                    eprintln!("error: {e}");
    80→                    ExitCode::FAILURE
    81→                }
    82→            }
    83→        }
    84→    }
    85→}
    86→
    87→#[cfg(test)]
    88→mod tests {
    89→    use super::*;
    90→
    91→    #[test]
    92→    fn unknown_command() {
    93→        let err = Cli::try_parse_from(["vc-x1", "bogus"])
    94→            .unwrap_err()
    95→            .to_string();
    96→        assert!(err.contains("bogus"));
    97→    }
    98→}
    99→

> AGENT

OK so these are 12-char IDs, just missing the path prefix. But you're saying the fix should also:

1. **Verify the changeID actually exists** in the other repo — confirm it resolves
2. **Count correctly** — the "3 fixed" is right but the dry-run says "skip" for ones that are already correct, which could mask ones that have the right format but wrong *value*

So the validation should be: load the other repo, resolve the changeID from the ochid trailer, and confirm it exists. If it doesn't resolve, flag it as an error.

Want me to add that verification? It would need the other repo path (not just the path prefix) — so maybe `--other-repo` pointing to the actual repo to validate against?

> DEVELOPER

Also, should verify the length of the ochid value is the "correct" value maybe another parameter?

> AGENT

Right. So the full validation:

1. **Path prefix** — must start with `--other-path`
2. **ID length** — changeID must be exactly N chars (default 12, configurable with e.g. `--id-len`)
3. **ID exists** — resolve against the other repo to confirm it's real

That means we need:
- `--other-path` — the prefix (e.g. `/.claude/` or `/`)
- `--other-repo` — path to the other repo for validation (e.g. `.claude` or `.`)
- `--id-len` — expected changeID length (default 12)

And the fix logic becomes: flag a commit if *any* of those three checks fail, not just the missing prefix. The dry-run output should show *why* each commit needs fixing (wrong prefix, wrong length, ID not found).

Sound right?

y

    11→pub struct FixOchidArgs {
