    34→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    35→}
    36→
    37→/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.
    38→///
    39→/// If the description has an `ochid:` line, ensure the value starts with `other_path`.
    40→/// If it already has a path prefix starting with `/`, replace it.
    41→/// If it's a bare changeID, prepend `other_path`.
    42→/// Normalizes the changeID to 12 characters.
    43→fn fix_ochid_in_description(desc: &str, other_path: &str, new_title: Option<&str>) -> String {
    44→    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
    45→
    46→    // Fix title if requested
    47→    if let Some(title) = new_title
    48→        && !lines.is_empty()
    49→    {
    50→        lines[0] = title.to_string();
    51→    }
    52→
    53→    // Fix ochid trailer
    54→    for line in &mut lines {
    55→        let trimmed = line.trim();
    56→        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
    57→            let value = value.trim();
    58→            // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)
    59→            let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {
    60→                &value[pos + 1..]
    61→            } else {
    62→                value
    63→            };
    64→            // Normalize to 12 chars
    65→            let short_id = &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(12)];
    66→            // Reconstruct with correct path
    67→            let prefix = if other_path.ends_with('/') {
    68→                other_path.to_string()
    69→            } else {
    70→                format!("{other_path}/")
    71→            };
    72→            *line = format!("ochid: {prefix}{short_id}");
    73→        }
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    // Preserve trailing newline if original had one
    77→    let mut result = lines.join("\n");
    78→    if desc.ends_with('\n') {
    79→        result.push('\n');
    80→    }
    81→    result
    82→}
    83→
    84→pub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    85→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    86→
    87→    let revset_str = if let Some(n) = args.limit {
    88→        format!("ancestors({}, {})", args.revision, n)
    89→    } else {
    90→        args.revision.clone()
    91→    };
    92→
    93→    let ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &revset_str)?;
    94→    if ids.is_empty() {
    95→        return Err(format!("no commits found for revision '{}'", args.revision).into());
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    99→    let mut fixed = 0;
   100→    let mut skipped = 0;
   101→
   102→    for commit_id in &ids {
   103→        if *commit_id == root_id {
   104→            continue;
   105→        }
   106→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   107→        let desc = commit.description();
   108→        let change_hex = jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   109→        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   110→
   111→        let new_desc = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, &args.other_path, args.title.as_deref());
   112→
   113→        if new_desc == desc {
   114→            skipped += 1;
   115→            if args.dry_run {
   116→                println!("skip {change_short}  (no change needed)");
   117→            }
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        if args.dry_run {
   122→            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   123→            println!("fix  {change_short}  {first_line}");
   124→            // Show the ochid line

   126→                if line.trim().starts_with("ochid:") {
   127→                    println!(
   128→                        "     ochid: {}",
   129→                        line.trim().strip_prefix("ochid:").unwrap().trim()
   130→                    );
   131→                }
   132→            }
   133→        } else {
   134→            let status = Command::new("jj")
   135→                .arg("describe")
   136→                .arg("-m")
   137→                .arg(&new_desc)
   138→                .arg("-r")
   139→                .arg(commit_id.hex())
   140→                .arg("-R")
   141→                .arg(&args.repo)
   142→                .arg("--ignore-immutable")
   143→                .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())
   144→                .stderr(std::process::Stdio::inherit())
   145→                .status()?;
   146→            if !status.success() {
   147→                return Err(format!(
   148→                    "jj describe failed for {} (exit {})",
   149→                    change_short,
   150→                    status.code().unwrap_or(-1)
   151→                )
   152→                .into());
   153→            }
   154→            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   155→            println!("fixed {change_short}  {first_line}");
   156→        }
   157→        fixed += 1;
   158→    }

   160→    println!(

All validating correctly:
- **ok** — valid ochid (right prefix, right length, ID found in other repo)

> DEVELOPER

I think we don't need two "repo" options, just one, the relative or full path to the other repo. Also our ".." syntax isn't valid, is there a reason we can use @.. that's quite natural?

> AGENT

Two good simplifications:

1. **Derive the path from `--other-repo`**: `.claude` → `/.claude/`, `.` → `/`. No need for a separate `--other-path`.

2. **Support `@..` notation**: Use `resolve_spec` + `collect_ids` like the other subcommands instead of raw revset strings.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/fix_ochid.rs",
  "content": "use std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::process::Command;\nuse std::sync::Arc;\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;\nuse jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};\nuse jj_lib::workspace::Workspace;\n\nuse crate::common;\n\nconst DEFAULT_ID_LEN: usize = 12;\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct FixOchidArgs {\n    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)\n    #[arg(value_name = \"REVISION\")]\n    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Number of commits (per dotted side)\n    #[arg(value_name = \"COMMITS\")]\n    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,\n\n    /// Revision(s) to fix\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Path to the other repo (e.g. .claude or .)\n    #[arg(long = \"other-repo\")]\n    pub other_repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Expected changeID length\n    #[arg(long = \"id-len\", default_value_t = DEFAULT_ID_LEN)]\n    pub id_len: usize,\n\n    /// New title to replace the first line (optional)\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub title: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Preview changes without writing\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub dry_run: bool,\n\n    /// Number of commits to fix\n    #[arg(short = 'n', long = \"commits\", value_name = \"COMMITS\")]\n    pub limit: Option<usize>,\n}\n\n/// Derive the ochid path prefix from a repo path.\n///\n/// `.` → `/`, `.claude` → `/.claude/`, `some/path` → `/some/path/`\nfn repo_to_ochid_prefix(repo_path: &PathBuf) -> String {\n    let s = repo_path.to_string_lossy();\n    if s == \".\" {\n        \"/\".to_string()\n    } else {\n        let trimmed = s.trim_end_matches('/');\n        format!(\"/{trimmed}/\")\n    }\n}\n\n/// Problems found with an ochid trailer.\n#[derive(Debug)]\nstruct OchidIssues {\n    wrong_prefix: bool,\n    wrong_length: bool,\n    not_found: bool,\n}\n\nimpl OchidIssues {\n    fn any(&self) -> bool {\n        self.wrong_prefix || self.wrong_length || self.not_found\n    }\n\n    fn summary(&self) -> String {\n        let mut parts = Vec::new();\n        if self.wrong_prefix {\n            parts.push(\"wrong prefix\");\n        }\n        if self.wrong_length {\n            parts.push(\"wrong ID length\");\n        }\n        if self.not_found {\n            parts.push(\"ID not found in other repo\");\n        }\n        parts.join(\", \")\n    }\n}\n\n/// Validate an ochid trailer value against expected prefix, length, and other repo.\nfn validate_ochid(\n    value: &str,\n    other_prefix: &str,\n    id_len: usize,\n    other_workspace: &Workspace,\n    other_repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n) -> OchidIssues {\n    let wrong_prefix = !value.starts_with(other_prefix);\n\n    // Extract the bare ID\n    let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {\n        &value[pos + 1..]\n    } else {\n        value\n    };\n\n    let wrong_length = bare_id.len() != id_len;\n\n    // Check if the ID resolves in the other repo\n    let not_found = common::resolve_revset(other_workspace, other_repo, bare_id)\n        .map(|ids| ids.is_empty())\n        .unwrap_or(true);\n\n    OchidIssues {\n        wrong_prefix,\n        wrong_length,\n        not_found,\n    }\n}\n\n/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.\nfn fix_ochid_in_description(\n    desc: &str,\n    other_prefix: &str,\n    id_len: usize,\n    new_title: Option<&str>,\n) -> String {\n    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();\n\n    // Fix title if requested\n    if let Some(title) = new_title\n        && !lines.is_empty()\n    {\n        lines[0] = title.to_string();\n    }\n\n    // Fix ochid trailer\n    for line in &mut lines {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"ochid:\") {\n            let value = value.trim();\n            // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)\n            let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {\n                &value[pos + 1..]\n            } else {\n                value\n            };\n            // Normalize to id_len chars\n            let short_id = &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(id_len)];\n            *line = format!(\"ochid: {other_prefix}{short_id}\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Preserve trailing newline if original had one\n    let mut result = lines.join(\"\\n\");\n    if desc.ends_with('\\n') {\n        result.push('\\n');\n    }\n    result\n}\n\npub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&args.other_repo)?;\n    let other_prefix = repo_to_ochid_prefix(&args.other_repo);\n\n    let spec = common::resolve_spec(\n        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n        args.pos_count,\n        &args.revision,\n        args.limit,\n        \"@\",\n    );\n\n    let (ids, _anchor_index) =\n        common::collect_ids(&workspace, &repo, &spec.rev, spec.desc_count, spec.anc_count)?;\n    if ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commits found for revision '{}'\", args.revision).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n    let mut fixed = 0;\n    let mut skipped = 0;\n    let mut errors = 0;\n\n    for commit_id in &ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        let desc = commit.description();\n        let change_hex = jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n\n        // Extract current ochid value\n        let current_ochid = common::extract_ochid(&commit);\n\n        // Validate current ochid\n        let issues = if let Some(ref ochid_val) = current_ochid {\n            validate_ochid(\n                ochid_val,\n                &other_prefix,\n                args.id_len,\n                &other_workspace,\n                &other_repo,\n            )\n        } else {\n            // No ochid trailer — nothing to fix\n            skipped += 1;\n            if args.dry_run {\n                println!(\"skip {change_short}  (no ochid trailer)\");\n            }\n            continue;\n        };\n\n        if !issues.any() {\n            skipped += 1;\n            if args.dry_run {\n                println!(\"ok   {change_short}  (valid)\");\n            }\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Build the fixed description\n        let new_desc =\n            fix_ochid_in_description(desc, &other_prefix, args.id_len, args.title.as_deref());\n\n        // Re-extract the fixed ochid value for post-validation\n        let fixed_ochid = {\n            let mut val = None;\n            for line in new_desc.lines() {\n                let trimmed = line.trim();\n                if let Some(v) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"ochid:\") {\n                    val = Some(v.trim().to_string());\n                }\n            }\n            val\n        };\n\n        let post_issues = if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {\n            validate_ochid(v, &other_prefix, args.id_len, &other_workspace, &other_repo)\n        } else {\n            OchidIssues {\n                wrong_prefix: false,\n                wrong_length: false,\n                not_found: true,\n            }\n        };\n\n        if post_issues.not_found {\n            errors += 1;\n            eprintln!(\n                \"err  {change_short}  ID not found in other repo (ochid: {})\",\n                fixed_ochid.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"?\")\n            );\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        if args.dry_run {\n            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n            println!(\n                \"fix  {change_short}  {first_line}  [{}]\",\n                issues.summary()\n            );\n            if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {\n                println!(\"     -> ochid: {v}\");\n            }\n        } else {\n            let status = Command::new(\"jj\")\n                .arg(\"describe\")\n                .arg(\"-m\")\n                .arg(&new_desc)\n                .arg(\"-r\")\n                .arg(commit_id.hex())\n                .arg(\"-R\")\n                .arg(&args.repo)\n                .arg(\"--ignore-immutable\")\n                .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())\n                .stderr(std::process::Stdio::inherit())\n                .status()?;\n            if !status.success() {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"jj describe failed for {} (exit {})\",\n                    change_short,\n                    status.code().unwrap_or(-1)\n                )\n                .into());\n            }\n            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n            println!(\"fixed {change_short}  {first_line}\");\n        }\n        fixed += 1;\n    }\n\n    println!(\n        \"\\n{fixed} fixed, {skipped} skipped, {errors} errors (of {} total)\",\n        ids.len()\n    );\n    if errors > 0 {\n        Err(format!(\"{errors} commit(s) could not be fixed\").into())\n    } else {\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn prefix_from_dot() {\n        assert_eq!(repo_to_ochid_prefix(&PathBuf::from(\".\")), \"/\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn prefix_from_claude() {\n        assert_eq!(repo_to_ochid_prefix(&PathBuf::from(\".claude\")), \"/.claude/\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn prefix_from_path() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            repo_to_ochid_prefix(&PathBuf::from(\"some/path\")),\n            \"/some/path/\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fix_bare_id() {\n        let desc = \"Some title\\n\\nBody text.\\n\\nochid: tzupykyyvnrp\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /.claude/tzupykyyvnrp\"));\n        assert!(result.starts_with(\"Some title\\n\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fix_wrong_prefix() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: /wrong/tzupykyyvnrp\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /.claude/tzupykyyvnrp\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn already_correct() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: /.claude/tzupykyyvnrp\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);\n        assert_eq!(result, desc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn truncate_long_id() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: abcdefghijklmnop\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /abcdefghijkl\"));\n        assert!(!result.contains(\"mnop\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fix_title_and_ochid() {\n        let desc = \"Old title\\n\\nochid: bare12345678\\n\";\n        let result =\n            fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, Some(\"New title\"));\n        assert!(result.starts_with(\"New title\\n\"));\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /.claude/bare12345678\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn no_ochid_no_change() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nNo trailer here.\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/.claude/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);\n        assert_eq!(result, desc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn other_path_without_trailing_slash() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: abcdefghijkl\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/\", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /abcdefghijkl\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn custom_id_len() {\n        let desc = \"Title\\n\\nochid: abcdefghijklmnop\\n\";\n        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, \"/\", 8, None);\n        assert!(result.contains(\"ochid: /abcdefgh\"));\n        assert!(!result.contains(\"ijkl\"));\n    }\n}\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

I want to do a full end to end, and I just thought of something, If I push these repos to repos with new names and then reload the renamed repos are the chid's going to be the same?

> AGENT

Yes — changeIDs are stored in git commit headers by jj. They survive push/clone/fetch because they're part of the commit data itself (in a `Change-Id:` header in the git commit object). So if you push to a new remote and clone it back, the changeIDs will be identical.

That's actually one of the key design properties of jj — changeIDs are immutable and transport-safe, unlike operation history and evolog which are local-only.

So a test plan like: push both repos to temp remotes, clone them fresh, run `fix-ochid` on the clones, verify — would work perfectly. The changeIDs will match.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about how Jujutsu stores change IDs and their preservation:

## Change ID Storage

Change IDs are stored in git commit headers as reverse hex encodings. This is important because it allows Jujutsu to track changes across Git operations.

## Preservation Across Git Operations

However, the preservation of change IDs is not universal. This is a non-standard header and is not preserved by all git tooling. For example, the header is preserved by a git commit --amend, but is not preserved through a rebase operation.

The good news is that GitHub and other major forges seem to preserve them for the most part.

## Regarding Clone Operations

While the search results don't explicitly address whether change IDs are preserved during `git clone` operations specifically, the fact that they're stored as standard git commit headers suggests they should be cloned along with the commits themselves, since cloning preserves the full commit metadata. However, the key limitation is that Git tooling that modifies commits (like rebase operations) may not preserve these non-standard headers.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Links: [{"title":"Tutorial and bird's eye view - Jujutsu docs","url":"https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/tutorial/"},{"title":"Releases · jj-vcs/jj","url":"https://github.com/jj-vcs/jj/releases"},{"title":"Git compatibility - Jujutsu docs","url":"https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/git-compatibility/"},{"title":"Settings - Jujutsu docs","url":"https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/config/"},{"title":"jj/CHANGELOG.md at main · jj-vcs/jj","url":"https://github.com/martinvonz/jj/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md"},{"title":"Common GitHub workflows result in divergent commits with `git.write-change-id-header = true` · Issue #6724 · jj-vcs/jj","url":"https://github.com/jj-vcs/jj/issues/6724"},{"title":"a jj cheat sheet for git users · GitHub","url":"https://gist.github.com/elithrar/4a09ef750af5624b729a6f1d87a0431c"},{"title":"Working with Gerrit - Jujutsu docs","url":"https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/gerrit/"},{"title":"Tutorial and Birds-Eye View - Jujutsu docs","url":"https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/v0.16.0/tutorial/"},{"title":"my jujutsu config · GitHub","url":"https://gist.github.com/thoughtpolice/8f2fd36ae17cd11b8e7bd93a70e31ad6"}]

I found relevant information about Jujutsu's Change-Id functionality. Here are the key findings:

## Change-Id in Jujutsu

Jujutsu has experimental support for transferring the change ID to/from Git remotes, enabled by default as of release 0.29.0. The change ID is stored in the Git commit itself in a commit header called "change-id", which means it will be transferred by regular git push etc.

## Git Commit Header Format

Change IDs are stored in git commit headers as reverse hex encodings. However, this is a non-standard header and is not preserved by all git tooling. For example, the header is preserved by a git commit --amend, but is not preserved through a rebase operation.

## Backend Configuration

This functionality is currently behind a git.write-change-id-header flag. You can disable it by setting git.write-change-id-header.

## Gerrit Integration

The template function format_gerrit_change_id_trailer(commit) creates a "Change-Id" trailer suitable to be used with Gerrit, based on Jujutsu's change id.

The search results show that while Jujutsu stores change IDs in git commit headers, this is an evolving feature with compatibility considerations across different Git tools and remotes.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

> AGENT

There it is — `change-id` is stored as a git commit header. Since `git.write-change-id-header = true` is set, it's written to every commit and **will survive push/clone** because it's part of the git commit object.

So yes — your changeIDs will be preserved when you push to a new remote and clone back. The test plan is safe.

Sources:
- [Git compatibility - Jujutsu docs](https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/git-compatibility/)
- [Common GitHub workflows result in divergent commits with `git.write-change-id-header = true` · Issue #6724](https://github.com/jj-vcs/jj/issues/6724)

> DEVELOPER

The last paragraph of the "Git compatibility - Jujutsu docs" says:
Change IDs are stored in git commit headers as reverse hex encodings. This is a non-standard header and is not preserved by all git tooling. For example, the header is preserved by a git commit --amend, but is not preserved through a rebase operation. GitHub and other major forges seem to preserve them for the most part. This functionality is currently behind a git.write-change-id-header flag.

So it is not guarantteed that the commit-id will be perserved!

> AGENT

You're right — "not preserved by all git tooling" is a real caveat. GitHub preserves them, but any git operation that rewrites commits (rebase, cherry-pick, squash via git) could strip the `change-id` header. A clone from GitHub should be safe, but it's not a universal guarantee.

So for your test plan — pushing to GitHub and cloning back should preserve the changeIDs. But if any intermediate tooling touches the commits (CI that rebases, GitHub merge squash, etc.), they could be lost.

Worth keeping in mind as we build tooling that depends on changeID cross-references being stable.

}

  "old_string": "to rewrite descriptions.",
  "new_string": "to rewrite descriptions.\n\n## Git commit headers and jj change-id preservation\n\n### How jj stores change IDs in git\n\njj stores the change ID as a non-standard git commit header called\n`change-id`. This is part of the raw commit object, visible with\n`git cat-file commit <hash>`:\n\n```\n$ git cat-file commit f22d6c45b9d9\ntree 55d1626ba2ad932a568fd669d18cc19eb38510fa\nparent 432b7494734a5bbe549cd796335d9e67756f07bc\nauthor Wink Saville <wink@saville.com> 1773950107 -0700\ncommitter Wink Saville <wink@saville.com> 1773950107 -0700\nchange-id mkoooktqrrqpllrkwxvosunuywplqsyq\n\nDeduplicate common CLI flags with #[command(flatten)] (0.21.1)\n\nExtract CommonArgs struct in common.rs shared by chid, desc, list, show.\nList adds width, show adds files on top. Update tests to access via .common.\n\nochid: /.claude/lwwsmvroqzly\n```\n\nThe `change-id` header sits alongside standard git headers (tree, parent,\nauthor, committer). The value is the full reverse-hex encoding of the\nchange ID — jj's short form (`mkoooktqrrqp`) is a 12-char prefix of\nthe full value (`mkoooktqrrqpllrkwxvosunuywplqsyq`).\n\n### Preservation guarantees\n\nFrom the [jj Git compatibility docs](https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/git-compatibility/):\n\n> Change IDs are stored in git commit headers as reverse hex encodings.\n> This is a non-standard header and is not preserved by all git tooling.\n> For example, the header is preserved by a `git commit --amend`, but is\n> not preserved through a rebase operation. GitHub and other major forges\n> seem to preserve them for the most part.\n\n**What preserves change IDs:**\n- `git push` / `git fetch` / `git clone` — the header is part of the\n  commit object, transferred as-is\n- `git commit --amend` — preserves non-standard headers\n- GitHub / major forges — preserve them in storage and API\n\n**What can strip change IDs:**\n- `git rebase` — rewrites commits, drops non-standard headers\n- `git cherry-pick` — creates new commits without the header\n- GitHub merge-squash / rebase-merge — creates new commits server-side\n\n### Configuration\n\nControlled by `git.write-change-id-header` (default `true` since jj 0.29.0).\nVerify with:\n\n```\n$ jj config get git.write-change-id-header\ntrue\n```\n\n### Implications for ochid cross-references\n\nSince our ochid trailers reference change IDs in the *other* repo, the\ncross-references are only as stable as the change-id headers in both repos.\nA regular `git push` to GitHub and `git clone` back will preserve them,\nbut any git tooling that rewrites commits could break the chain."
}

The correct invocation from project root should be:

```
vc-x1 validate-desc -R .claude . @..
```

> AGENT

Works. The earlier `.. @..` failed because `..` was parsed as the `OTHER_REPO` path (parent directory), not as revision notation. The fix is just argument ordering — `OTHER_REPO` must come before the positional revision.

> DEVELOPER

The problem is if "other-repo" is a relative path it relative to the current cwd at the time of invocation, so if I cd into .claude the command is `vc-x1 validate-desc .. @..` but if I'm in the root the "other-repo" is ".", a little weird. The rule is all relative paths are relative to the cwd at time of execution, correct

> AGENT

Yes, that's correct — relative paths are always relative to cwd at invocation time. So:

- From project root: `vc-x1 validate-desc -R .claude . @..` (other repo is `.` = project root)
- From `.claude/`: `vc-x1 validate-desc .. @..` (other repo is `..` = project root)

The `..` ambiguity is unfortunate — `..` as a path vs `..` as revision notation. But clap resolves it by position: first positional is always `OTHER_REPO`, second is `REVISION`. So `.. @..` works: `..` is the path, `@..` is the revision.

It's a bit confusing visually but functionally correct. The `-R` flag approach from project root is cleaner and what you'd normally use.

> DEVELOPER

Well it's what you normally use, but I have multiple terminals open so I tend not to use -R at all!

> AGENT

Good point — if you're already cd'd into the repo you want to operate on, `-R` is redundant. The natural invocation is just:

```
# From .claude/
vc-x1 validate-desc .. @..

# From project root
vc-x1 validate-desc .claude @..
```

Both clean, no `-R` needed. The `-R` is really only for the multi-repo case or when the bot needs to target a repo without cd'ing.

> DEVELOPER

yep, and i was testing from root, which I normally don't do, so  I wasn't sure and made the wrong assumption!

> AGENT

Makes sense — from root, `.claude` is the other repo, not `.`. Easy to mix up when you're used to being inside the repo you're operating on.

Anything else to do in this session, or are we ready to commit and finalize?

The key info is that it's unfixable and why. How about keeping it concise:

```

That tells you: the ID is gone, no title match to infer from, and what to do about it. The length issue is secondary since the root cause is "not found".

```
err  zssouppsmspu  Refactor finalize into src/finalize.rs (0.7.0-dev2)  [not found: ouoyxokq, unfixable without --fallback]
```

wink@3900x 26-03-20T22:19:49.789Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude ((main))


But are they ok if without the snapshot?

> AGENT

The snapshot warnings are just jj refusing to add large session files to the working copy snapshot — it doesn't affect `jj describe` which only rewrites commit metadata. The fixes went through (22 fixed, all validate as ok now).

The "Rebased N descendant commits" messages are expected — when you rewrite an older commit's description, jj rebases all descendants to maintain the DAG. The changeIDs stay the same but commitIDs change.

The snapshot issue is a separate concern — those large `.jsonl` files should probably be in `.gitignore` for the `.claude` repo. But it didn't block the fix-desc operation.

Did you want to proceed with fixing the app repo too? That one has the 3 lost IDs needing `--fallback`:

```
vc-x1 fix-desc .claude @.. --add-missing --fallback /.claude/lost --no-dry-run
```

> DEVELOPER

That sounds reassuring, but my goal is that "all" information in claude should be "preserved" so when I want to create the chorent story we have all the information. Soooooo, are all snapshots recreatable and I should just ignore them?

> AGENT

The snapshots are just jj's working copy snapshots — they capture uncommitted file changes into a temporary commit. When jj says "Refused to snapshot some files," it means those large `.jsonl` session files won't be captured in the *current* working copy snapshot. But:

1. The session data in those `.jsonl` files is the *current live conversation* — it gets committed at session end via finalize
2. The `jj describe` operations only touch commit metadata (descriptions), not file content
3. All previously committed session data is safe in the commit history

So the large file warnings are about *this session's* uncommitted conversation data, not about historical data. Finalize will capture it at the end.

The question is: will increasing the snapshot size limit lose anything? No — it just lets jj snapshot larger files. You could run `jj config set --repo snapshot.max-new-file-size 4489522` in `.claude` to stop the warnings, but it's not required for fix-desc to work correctly.

Your historical session data is preserved in commits. The fix-desc only changed description text, not file trees.

> DEVELOPER

wink@3900x 26-03-20T22:30:20.015Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude ((main))


as well the first sub-directory which also has a file and data in the directory

> AGENT

All of this is real session data — every UUID is a Claude Code conversation session. The `.jsonl` files are conversation transcripts and the directories contain subagent data (like the Explore/Plan agents we've used). None of it is "snapshot" data that can be regenerated.

The jj "snapshot" warnings are about jj's *working copy snapshot mechanism* — jj automatically snapshots uncommitted files into a temp commit so you can undo. It's not a separate file type. The warning just means jj won't track those large files in the working copy until you increase the limit or commit them via finalize.

So: all files in `.claude/` are real conversation data worth preserving. The large ones that jj refuses to snapshot are just current/recent sessions that grew past the 1.4MB default limit. Finalize handles committing them at session end — you could bump the limit with:

```
jj config set --repo snapshot.max-new-file-size 5000000 -R .claude
```

to stop the warnings and ensure jj can snapshot everything.

> DEVELOPER

I'm not worried about the warnings per se, and increased it from the default to 1.4M you see now and it's want's more. Do you know the consequence.  If I add them to .gitignore they won't be saved to github and and that implies to me we'l lose data, thoughts?

> AGENT

You're right — `.gitignore` would mean those files never get committed, so they'd be lost on push. Don't do that.

The consequence of increasing `snapshot.max-new-file-size` is just: jj will include larger files in its automatic working copy snapshots. This means:

1. **More data in jj's undo history** — each snapshot creates a temp commit, so large files get stored more often. Slightly more disk usage in `.jj/`.
2. **Slower snapshots** — jj hashes the file contents each time. A 4.5MB file is negligible though.
3. **No data loss risk** — it's the safe direction. You're *allowing* jj to track more, not less.

The only downside of a low limit is exactly what you're seeing — jj silently skips large files, which means if finalize doesn't run (or fails), those files are untracked and could be lost.

I'd bump it generously:

```
jj config set --repo snapshot.max-new-file-size 10000000 -R .claude
```

10MB gives plenty of headroom. Session files grow with conversation length but shouldn't exceed that.

503-
504-## 0.23.0 — Add --add-missing to fix-ochid
505-
506-New `--add-missing` flag for `fix-ochid` that adds ochid trailers to commits
507-that don't have one. Matches commits by title (exact) and committer timestamp
508-(within 60 seconds). Only adds the trailer when exactly one match is found in
509-the other repo — ambiguous or zero matches are skipped. Respects `--no-dry-run`.
510-
511-## 0.25.0 — Refactor into validate-desc / fix-desc
512-
513-### 0.25.0-dev1 — Add validate-desc, extract desc_helpers
514-
515-Extract shared helpers into `desc_helpers.rs`: `ochid_prefix_from_config`,
516-`OchidIssues`, `validate_ochid`, `find_matching_commit`, `fix_ochid_in_description`,
517-`append_ochid_trailer`, `resolve_full_change_id`, `extract_bare_id`,
518-`extract_ochid_from_desc`. New `validate-desc` subcommand: read-only scan
519-reporting ok/err/miss/skip status per commit. Supports `--check-missing` to
520-report potential matches by title+timestamp. `fix-ochid` rewritten to use
521-`desc_helpers` with no duplicated logic.
522-
523:### 0.25.0-dev2 — Add fix-desc subcommand
524-
525-New `fix-desc` subcommand using shared `desc_helpers`. Same positional arg
526-interface as `validate-desc` (`OTHER_REPO` required positional). Adds
527-`--no-dry-run`, `--add-missing`, `--max-fixes`, `--fallback`, `--title`.
528-Extracted `jj_describe` helper to reduce duplication. Marked `fix-ochid`
529-as deprecated in help text.
530-
531-#### Matching and error reporting improvements
532-
533-Switched from timestamp+title matching to title-only matching. The original
534-60-second timestamp tolerance was too strict — committer timestamps shift on
535-`jj squash`, `jj describe`, and `jj rebase`, so commits made together can
536-diverge by minutes. Title-only matching is more robust.
537-
538-When multiple commits share the same title, the match is ambiguous and
539-reported as `[N title matches, ambiguous]` — no auto-fix without an
540-explicit override (future feature).
541-
542-`OchidIssues` now carries actual values (not just bools) for verbose error
543-reporting:
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-02.md",
  "old_string": "`TitleMatch` enum replaces `Option<String>` return from `find_matching_commit`,\ndistinguishing `NoTitle`, `One(id)`, `Ambiguous(n)`, and `None`.\n\n## 0.24.0 — Add --max-fixes to fix-ochid",
  "new_string": "`TitleMatch` enum replaces `Option<String>` return from `find_matching_commit`,\ndistinguishing `NoTitle`, `One(id)`, `Ambiguous(n)`, and `None`.\n\n#### Special ochid values: `lost` and `none`\n\n`validate-desc` recognizes two special ochid bare IDs:\n- `lost` — the counterpart commit was lost (e.g. `.claude` history squashed\n  incorrectly). Set via `fix-desc --fallback /.claude/lost`. Status: `lost`.\n- `none` — no counterpart exists (human-only change, no bot involvement).\n  Set manually. Status: `none`.\n\nBoth are treated as intentional non-error states — they don't count as\n`issues` and don't trigger the error exit code.\n\n#### Improved error messages\n\nError summaries use introducer+semicolon format for readability:\n`[short ochid; len: 8 (want 12), not found; /.claude/ouoyxokq, consider using; --fallback of fix-desc]`\n\nThe `not found` message now includes the full ochid path (not just\nthe bare ID) and suggests the `--fallback` flag as a remedy.\n\n## 0.24.0 — Add --max-fixes to fix-ochid"
}

> DEVELOPER

Before we make this 0.25.0 I think we can improve these two we don't need to provide the other-repo it's in .vc-config.toml, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good idea — each repo's `.vc-config.toml` already has `workspace.path` which identifies it. We could add a field like `workspace.other` or `workspace.other_repo` to point to the counterpart repo. Then `validate-desc` and `fix-desc` could read it automatically, making `OTHER_REPO` optional with a fallback to the config.

Or even simpler — since the dual-repo setup is always `.` and `.claude`, we could infer: if the current repo's path is `/.claude/`, the other is `.`; if it's `/`, the other is `.claude`. But that's fragile if the layout changes.

Which approach do you prefer — config field or inference?

> DEVELOPER

I prefer config field. And it doens't need to be named .claude that just a suggested conventiion, so we should verify that it's not assumed anywhere in the code.

> AGENT

Good point. Let me check if `.claude` is hardcoded anywhere in the source.

> TOOL

> AGENT

All `.claude` references are in:
- **Help text / doc comments** — `(e.g. .claude or .)` — just examples, fine
- **Tests** — using `.claude` as a test value for `-R` — fine, it's just a path string
- **desc_helpers/fix_ochid tests** — using `/.claude/` as test ochid prefix — fine, test data

No hardcoded assumptions that the other repo *must* be named `.claude`. The prefix is always derived from `.vc-config.toml` at runtime.

For the config field, I'd add `workspace.other_repo` to `.vc-config.toml`:

```toml
[workspace]
path = "/"
other_repo = ".claude"
```

and in `.claude/.vc-config.toml`:

```toml
[workspace]
path = "/.claude"
other_repo = "."
```

Then `validate-desc` and `fix-desc` could make `OTHER_REPO` optional — read from config if not provided. Want me to implement this as the next step toward 0.25.0?

> DEVELOPER

Make reintroduce `--other-repo <OTHER_REPO>`

> AGENT

So `OTHER_REPO` becomes optional positional — if not provided, read from `.vc-config.toml`'s `workspace.other_repo`. And `--other-repo` is reintroduced as a flag override.

That gives three ways to specify it (in precedence order):
1. `--other-repo .claude` (flag, highest)
2. `vc-x1 validate-desc .claude @..` (positional)
3. `.vc-config.toml` `workspace.other_repo` (config default)

Or did you mean drop the positional entirely and only use `--other-repo` flag + config?

> DEVELOPER

no not positional at all it's not a common thing like count or revset

> AGENT

Makes sense — `--other-repo` flag only, with config fallback. So:

- `vc-x1 validate-desc @..` — reads other repo from `.vc-config.toml`
- `vc-x1 validate-desc --other-repo .claude @..` — explicit override
- No positional `OTHER_REPO`

Clean and consistent. Let me implement this. First bump version.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/validate_desc.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn validate_desc(args: &ValidateDescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&args.other_repo)?;\n    let other_config = toml_simple::toml_load(&args.other_repo.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;\n    let other_prefix = ochid_prefix_from_config(&other_config)?;",
  "new_string": "pub fn validate_desc(args: &ValidateDescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    // Resolve other repo: --other-repo flag, or fall back to .vc-config.toml\n    let other_repo_path = if let Some(ref p) = args.other_repo {\n        p.clone()\n    } else {\n        let config = toml_simple::toml_load(&args.repo.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;\n        PathBuf::from(other_repo_from_config(&config)?)\n    };\n\n    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&other_repo_path)?;\n    let other_config = toml_simple::toml_load(&other_repo_path.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;\n    let other_prefix = ochid_prefix_from_config(&other_config)?;"
}
```

  "new_string": "pub fn fix_desc(args: &FixDescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    // Resolve other repo: --other-repo flag, or fall back to .vc-config.toml\n    let other_repo_path = if let Some(ref p) = args.other_repo {\n        p.clone()\n    } else {\n        let config = toml_simple::toml_load(&args.repo.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;\n        PathBuf::from(other_repo_from_config(&config)?)\n    };\n\n    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&other_repo_path)?;\n    let other_config = toml_simple::toml_load(&other_repo_path.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;\n    let other_prefix = ochid_prefix_from_config(&other_config)?;"
}
```

113 tests pass, clean clippy. Both `validate-desc` and `fix-desc` now read `workspace.other-repo` from `.vc-config.toml` by default, with `--other-repo` as an override. Ready for your review.

> DEVELOPER

Why is the default in square brackets for other-repoand maybe --no-dry-run?

> AGENT

The `--other-repo` default shows no `[default: ...]` because it's `Option<PathBuf>` with no `default_value` — clap only shows defaults for fields that have one. The help text says "(default: from .vc-config.toml)" manually, which is correct since it's a runtime fallback, not a clap default.

For `--no-dry-run` — it's a boolean flag, clap doesn't show `[default: false]` for those.

Were you asking why they *don't* have square brackets? Or did you want me to add something?

> DEVELOPER

I'll KIS, why is the default in () and not []: "--other-repo <OTHER_REPO>  Path to the other repo (default: from .vc-config.toml)"

> AGENT

Good catch — should be `[default: from .vc-config.toml]` for consistency.

```
   186→### validate-desc
   187→
   188→Read-only scan of commit descriptions against the other repo. Reports
   189→status per commit: `ok` (valid ochid), `lost` (ochid: lost), `none`
   190→(ochid: none), `err` (issues found), or `miss` (no ochid trailer).
   191→Always checks for potential matches by title in the other repo.
   192→
   193→```
   194→# Validate all ancestors of @
   195→vc-x1 validate-desc .claude @..
   196→
   197→# Validate specific range
   198→vc-x1 validate-desc .claude -r @.. -n 20
   199→```
   200→
   201→Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
   202→
   203→### fix-desc
   204→
   205→Fix commit descriptions against the other repo. Default is dry-run —
   206→use `--no-dry-run` to write changes. Same positional args as
   207→`validate-desc`.
   208→
   209→```
   210→# Dry-run: show what would be fixed
   211→vc-x1 fix-desc .claude @..
   212→
   213→# Actually fix
   214→vc-x1 fix-desc .claude @.. --no-dry-run
   215→
   216→# Add missing ochid trailers by matching title + timestamp
   217→vc-x1 fix-desc .claude @.. --add-missing
   218→
   219→# Limit fixes
   220→vc-x1 fix-desc .claude @.. --no-dry-run -m 3
   221→
   222→# Use a fallback for IDs not found in other repo
   223→vc-x1 fix-desc .claude @.. --fallback /.claude/lost
   224→```
   225→
   226→| Flag | Description |
   227→|------|-------------|
   228→| `--no-dry-run` | Write fixes (default is dry-run) |
   229→| `--add-missing` | Infer and add ochid for commits without one |
   230→| `-m, --max-fixes <N>` | Stop fixing after N commits changed [default: all] |
   231→| `--fallback <VALUE>` | Replacement for IDs not found in other repo |
   232→| `--id-len <N>` | Expected changeID length (default 12) |
   233→| `--title <TEXT>` | Replace commit title at the same time |
   234→
   235→Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
```

Options:

  -n, --commits <COMMITS>        Number of commits to scan
  -R, --repo <REPO>              Path to jj repo [default: .]

$ vc-x1 validate-desc --help

Arguments:

          Revision (with optional .. notation)

Options:

          Revision(s) to scan
          
          [default: @]

  -n, --commits <COMMITS>

  -R, --repo <REPO>
          Path to jj repo

      --other-repo <OTHER_REPO>
          Path to the other repo [default: from .vc-config.toml]

          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
wink@3900x 26-03-21T01:37:13.377Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1 ((main))

> AGENT

Yes, that's a clap thing. With `-h` (short help) clap puts `[default: ...]` inline on the same line. With `--help` (long help) it puts clap-managed defaults on a separate line below the description. But our manual `[default: from .vc-config.toml]` in the doc comment stays inline because clap doesn't know it's a default — it's just part of the description text.

No clean way to fix it without either accepting the inconsistency or dropping the manual default hints. Since clap can't show a runtime-resolved default, the inline text is the best we can do.

Ready to commit?

> DEVELOPER

y

     1→mod chid;
     2→mod common;
     3→mod desc;
     4→mod desc_helpers;
     5→mod finalize;

     9→mod show;
    10→mod toml_simple;
    11→mod validate_desc;
    12→
    13→use std::process::ExitCode;
    14→
    15→use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};
    16→
    17→#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
    18→#[command(version, about = "vc-x1: jj workspace tooling")]
    19→pub struct Cli {
    20→    #[command(subcommand)]
    21→    pub(crate) command: Commands,
    22→}
    23→
    24→#[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
    25→pub(crate) enum Commands {
    26→    /// Print the changeID for a revision
    27→    Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
    28→
    29→    /// Show full description of a commit
    30→    Desc(desc::DescArgs),
    31→
    32→    /// List commits in a jj repo
    33→    List(list::ListArgs),
    34→
    35→    /// Show commit details and diff summary
    36→    Show(show::ShowArgs),
    37→
    38→    /// Validate commit descriptions against the other repo
    39→    #[command(
    40→        long_about = "Validate commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
    41→        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
    42→        Status labels:\n  \
    43→          ok   — ochid trailer is valid\n  \
    44→          err  — ochid has issues (wrong prefix, wrong length, ID not found)\n  \
    45→          miss — no ochid trailer; shows match from other repo if found"
    46→    )]
    47→    ValidateDesc(validate_desc::ValidateDescArgs),
    48→
    49→    /// Fix commit descriptions against the other repo (dry-run by default)
    50→    #[command(long_about = "Fix commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
    51→        Default is dry-run; use --no-dry-run to write changes.\n\n\
    52→        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
    53→        Status labels:\n  \
    54→          ok    — ochid trailer is valid (no change)\n  \
    55→          fix   — ochid has issues, shows proposed fix (dry-run)\n  \
    56→          fixed — ochid was rewritten (--no-dry-run)\n  \
    57→          add   — missing ochid, match found, shows proposed addition (dry-run)\n  \
    58→          added — missing ochid was added (--no-dry-run)\n  \
    59→          skip  — skipped (no ochid, no match, or max-fixes reached)\n  \
    60→          err   — ID not found and no --fallback provided")]
    61→    FixDesc(fix_desc::FixDescArgs),
    62→
    63→    /// Fix ochid trailers in commit descriptions (deprecated, use fix-desc)
    64→    FixOchid(fix_ochid::FixOchidArgs),
    65→
    66→    /// Squash working copy into target commit
    67→    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),
    68→}
    69→
    70→fn main() -> ExitCode {
    71→    let cli = Cli::parse();
    72→
    73→    match cli.command {
    74→        Commands::Chid(args) => {
    75→            if let Err(e) = chid::chid(&args) {
    76→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    77→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    78→            }
    79→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    80→        }
    81→        Commands::Desc(args) => {
    82→            if let Err(e) = desc::desc(&args) {
    83→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    84→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    85→            }
    86→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    87→        }
    88→        Commands::List(args) => {
    89→            if let Err(e) = list::list(&args) {
    90→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    91→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    92→            }
    93→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
    94→        }
    95→        Commands::Show(args) => {
    96→            if let Err(e) = show::show(&args) {
    97→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
    98→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
    99→            }
   100→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
   101→        }
   102→        Commands::ValidateDesc(args) => {
   103→            if let Err(e) = validate_desc::validate_desc(&args) {
   104→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
   105→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
   106→            }
   107→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
   108→        }
   109→        Commands::FixDesc(args) => {
   110→            if let Err(e) = fix_desc::fix_desc(&args) {
   111→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
   112→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
   113→            }
   114→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
   115→        }
   116→        Commands::FixOchid(args) => {
   117→            if let Err(e) = fix_ochid::fix_ochid(&args) {
   118→                eprintln!("error: {e}");
   119→                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
   120→            }
   121→            ExitCode::SUCCESS
   122→        }
   123→        Commands::Finalize(args) => {
   124→            let opts = args.into_opts();
   125→            finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize entry");
   126→            match finalize::finalize(&opts) {
   127→                Ok(()) => {
   128→                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, "main: finalize exit ok");
   129→                    ExitCode::SUCCESS
   130→                }
   131→                Err(e) => {
   132→                    finalize::log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("main: finalize exit err={e}"));
   133→                    eprintln!("error: {e}");
   134→                    ExitCode::FAILURE
   135→                }
   136→            }
   137→        }
   138→    }
   139→}
   140→
   141→#[cfg(test)]
   142→mod tests {
   143→    use super::*;
   144→
   145→    #[test]
   146→    fn unknown_command() {

   148→            .unwrap_err()
   149→            .to_string();
   150→        assert!(err.contains("bogus"));
   151→    }
   152→}
   153→

   238→Use `--help` for the full status label legend.
   239→
   240→### fix-ochid
   241→
   242→> **Deprecated**: use `validate-desc` and `fix-desc` instead.
   243→
   244→
   245→
   246→Validates and fixes ochid trailers across commit history. Checks three
   247→properties: correct path prefix, correct changeID length, and that the
   248→ID resolves in the other repo. Default is dry-run — use `--no-dry-run`
   249→to write changes.
   250→
   251→```
   252→# Dry-run: show what would be fixed
   253→vc-x1 fix-ochid -r @.. --other-repo .claude
   254→
   255→# Actually fix
   256→vc-x1 fix-ochid -r @.. --other-repo .claude --no-dry-run
   257→
   258→# Add missing ochid trailers by matching title + timestamp (within 60s)
   259→vc-x1 fix-ochid -r @.. --other-repo .claude --add-missing
   260→
   261→# Use a fallback for IDs not found in other repo
   262→vc-x1 fix-ochid -r @.. --other-repo .claude --fallback /.claude/lost
   263→```
   264→
   265→Key flags:
   266→
   267→| Flag | Description |
   268→|------|-------------|
   269→| `--other-repo <PATH>` | Path to the counterpart repo (required) |
   270→| `--no-dry-run` | Write fixes (default is dry-run) |
   271→| `--add-missing` | Infer and add ochid for commits without one |
   272→| `--fallback <VALUE>` | Replacement for IDs not found in other repo |
   273→| `--id-len <N>` | Expected changeID length (default 12) |
   274→| `--max-fixes <N>` | Stop fixing after N commits changed |
   275→| `--title <TEXT>` | Replace commit title at the same time |
   276→
   277→The `--add-missing` flag matches commits by exact title and committer
```

   277→The `--add-missing` flag matches commits by exact title and committer
   278→timestamp within 60 seconds. It only adds the trailer when exactly one
   279→match is found — ambiguous or zero matches are skipped.
   280→
   281→### finalize
   282→
   283→Solves the session repo coherence problem: when the bot commits `.claude`,
   284→the act of committing generates more session data. `finalize` waits,
   285→then squashes the trailing writes into the target commit.
   286→

   569→
   570→### 0.25.0-dev3 — Read other-repo from config
   571→
   572→`validate-desc` and `fix-desc` no longer require the other repo as a
   573→positional argument. Instead, they read `workspace.other-repo` from
   574→`.vc-config.toml` by default. The `--other-repo` flag overrides the
   575→config value. Added `other_repo_from_config()` to `desc_helpers`.
   576→Updated both `.vc-config.toml` files with `other-repo` field.
   577→Consistent `[default: ...]` square bracket style in all help text.
   578→
   579→## 0.24.0 — Add --max-fixes to fix-ochid
   580→
   581→New `--max-fixes` flag limits the number of commits actually changed.
   582→Scanning continues past the limit (reporting `skip ... (max-fixes reached)`),
   583→so the summary still shows the full picture. Applies to both regular fixes
